The Caddyfile **overwrites** `X-Forwarded-For` with the real client (`{remote_host}`) so the per‑tablet PIN lockout in [ratelimit.js](src/lib/ratelimit.js) (which reads the first XFF entry) can't be fed a forged value — and as a bonus it upgrades that lockout from a single shared `cred:ip:unknown` bucket (direct `next start` sets no XFF) to per‑tablet buckets, and **strips any inbound `x-dreamy-zone`** so the LAN path is always treated as LAN. The **till kiosk is unaffected** — [desktop/main.js](desktop/main.js) loads `http://127.0.0.1:3000`, and Chromium treats loopback as a secure context, so `Secure` cookies work there without TLS — and the **public tunnel is untouched** (still `cloudflared → localhost:3000`, TLS at the Cloudflare edge; it does **not** route through Caddy, so the `x-dreamy-zone` Transform Rule stays on the direct path). Delivered as: a repo-root [`Caddyfile`](../Caddyfile); [`scripts/install-caddy-service.ps1`](scripts/install-caddy-service.ps1) which registers a `DreamyCafeCaddy` NSSM service (auto-start, depends on `DreamyCafeApp`, log rotation — mirroring the existing services installer), adds firewall rules (**allow 443/80, block 3000 to the LAN** so tablets must use TLS; loopback is unfiltered so kiosk/Caddy/cloudflared keep working), and exports Caddy's root CA for the tablets; and a [docs/lan-tls.md](docs/lan-tls.md) walkthrough including the fiddly two-part iOS trust (install profile **and** enable it under Certificate Trust Settings). The one app change is a **single env-gated line** in [auth.js](src/lib/auth.js): `secure: process.env.SESSION_COOKIE_SECURE === '1'` — default off, so behaviour is unchanged until the operator sets `SESSION_COOKIE_SECURE=1` in `.env` **after** every tablet is on HTTPS (a `Secure` cookie is dropped on a still-plaintext tablet, silently breaking login there — sequencing is called out in the guide and reversible without a rebuild). Also flips on a **bonus**: the service worker only registers in a secure context, so the tills gain the offline app-shell once they're on `https://`. **Verification is operator-run on the shop hardware** — the plan's end-to-end checklist (padlock + login on an iPad, plaintext `:3000` refused, public zone still redirecting `/pos`→`/order`, kiosk still working, then enabling the Secure cookie and re-verifying login) lives in [docs/lan-tls.md](docs/lan-tls.md); no code path in the app changed except the env-gated cookie flag, which is inert until switched on. Primary control remains network segregation (POS on its own VLAN/SSID); this is the defence-in-depth that closes the plaintext-sniffing gap if a foothold is ever gained.

### Changed
- **Selecting a cashier is now optional instead of mandatory on both the Checkout and Quick Sale modals.** Checkout blocked Confirm until a cashier was chosen (red `*`, a "Select a cashier to continue" error, and a `!!cashier` gate on `canConfirm`); Quick Sale silently disabled its Cash and Card buttons via a `!!cashier` gate on `canCharge`. Shops that don't track per-cashier sales had to pick one every sale for no benefit. [CheckoutModal](src/components/pos/CheckoutModal.js) and [QuickSaleModal](src/components/pos/QuickSaleModal.js) now label Cashier `(optional)`, drop the blocking message, and no longer require a cashier to complete a sale — both payloads already sent `userId: cashier?.id ?? null`, so an order with no cashier saves cleanly and staff who do want attribution can still pick one. UI only — no money, payment, auth, or zone logic touched.

### Security
- **The LAN was treated as trusted, but the shop Wi-Fi *is* the POS network — so any customer's phone could read sales, forge cash movements, rewrite menu prices, and brute-force a 4-digit admin PIN. LAN APIs are now default-deny, and every credential endpoint has a failed-attempt lockout.** A security audit of the whole app found the internet-facing side solid (default-deny public allowlist, both webhooks signature-verified, no XSS sinks, parameterized SQL, traversal-safe uploads, bcrypt PINs, fail-fast `JWT_SECRET`) but the LAN side effectively unauthenticated, with a complete unauthenticated chain to full Owner access: `GET /api/users/list` returns every staff member's id, name **and role** (it has to — the login picker renders from it), and *all four* PIN endpoints plus `clock-inout` had **no rate limiting and no lockout**, so an attacker could pick the ADMIN off the list and grind all 10,000 four-digit combinations against `/api/auth/admin`, which mints an admin session directly. Changing the seed PINs does not fix this — the keyspace is 10,000 whatever the digits are; only limiting the attempt rate does. Fixes: **(1)** [proxy.js](src/proxy.js)'s LAN branch no longer waves every `/api/*` request through — it now requires a staff session (`hasStaffSession`: POS **or** manager **or** admin, since the Owner can enter `/admin` by PIN without ever starting a POS session) and falls back to 401, with a small `LAN_BOOTSTRAP_API` allowlist for the routes that must answer *before* a session exists (`/api/auth/*`, `/api/users/list`), carry their own proof of origin (`/api/webhooks/*`, signature-verified — listed as a safety net so a tunnel misconfiguration can't start rejecting payment callbacks), or serve infrastructure (`/api/health`, `/api/uploads/*`). Gating in the proxy rather than per-route means the ~30 unauthenticated staff routes are covered by one change *and* a newly added route is protected by default instead of opt-in — the previous page-level gates protected the pages but not the APIs behind them, so `curl` walked straight past them. **(2)** New failed-credential lockout in [ratelimit.js](src/lib/ratelimit.js) (5 failures → 15 min), applied to all five credential endpoints. It keys on the caller's IP **and** the targeted userId together, so an attacker can neither rotate IPs to keep hammering one account nor sit on one IP and spray every account; a miss on the user lookup counts as a failure too (otherwise userId spraying never trips the IP lock — caught when a live test accidentally targeted a nonexistent id and sailed past 7 attempts). Counts failures only, so a success clears the tally and ordinary mistyping never accumulates; the window is not extended by further attempts, so a locked-out staff member is guaranteed back in after 15 minutes rather than pushed out further by their own retries. **(3)** Server-side PIN format validation ([pinAttempts.js](src/lib/pinAttempts.js)) — `PIN_LENGTH` was a client-side constant only, so a direct API call could send anything; the rejection is deliberately indistinguishable from a wrong PIN. **Verified against a running server, not just the diff**: 11 staff routes (incl. `POST /api/cash/movements`, `POST /api/terminal/charge`, `PUT /api/products/[id]`) all 401 without a session; `/api/users/list`, `/api/health`, `/login` still 200; 5 wrong admin PINs → 6th returns **429**; the IP lock carries across endpoints (a spray against `/api/auth/manager` was refused immediately); a 9-digit PIN is rejected; and the full legitimate flow still works end-to-end — POS login 200 → staff APIs 200 → Admin PIN 200 → `/api/feature-settings` 200 — with 4 mistypes followed by a success correctly resetting the counter. 8 new unit tests (59/59 pass), build clean. **Not changed and still open:** session cookies have no `secure` flag (deliberate — the LAN runs over plain HTTP; revisit only if a session is ever issued on the public zone), no CSP/HSTS/`X-Frame-Options` headers are set, and `npm audit` reports 8 transitive vulns (2 critical, both via the unmaintained `escpos → get-pixels → request` chain; low practical exposure). Dropping `role` from `/api/users/list` was considered and rejected — [ManagerPinModal](src/components/shared/ManagerPinModal.js) filters the picker by it and the login screen labels Owner/Manager with it, and hiding it buys little once attempts are limited. The highest-leverage remaining fix is not code: **put the POS on a separate VLAN/SSID from customer Wi-Fi.**

### Fixed
- **The `FeatureSettingsContext` refactor made feature flags fresh within a single tab but stale across the shop's other tablets; it now polls so a toggle on one device reaches the rest, and several sharp edges around it are closed.** A max-effort review of the refactor found that consolidating five independent fetchers into one context removed every *organic* refresh the old code relied on (BottomNav/admin re-read on `session-changed`, CheckoutModal re-fetched on every modal open, manager-menu on every save) and replaced them with a **one-shot load per tab** — so on the multi-tablet LAN POS, a flag flipped on the office tablet stayed invisible on every already-open till until a full page reload. Worse corollary: a still-floor-plan till, unaware the shop had switched to Typed numbers, would let a cashier pick a table and save an order whose `tableId` the server then nulled — leaving the stand occupied on the floor but unmarked, and the docket printing a bare number instead of "Table N". Fixes: **(1)** [FeatureSettingsContext](src/contexts/FeatureSettingsContext.js) now refreshes via `usePollWhenVisible` (30s, the same visibility-gated hook every sibling context uses) instead of a one-shot fetch with a hand-rolled 15s failure-retry — cross-device changes propagate within a tick, backgrounded tabs pause, and there's no unbounded fixed-interval retry. **(2)** `apply()` (the same-tab PATCH fast-path) now aborts any in-flight GET before writing, so a slower background response can't land afterwards and silently revert an admin's just-saved toggle. **(3)** `/privacy` — a public, internet-facing page — was **not** in `isCustomerPath`, so the provider treated it as staff and fired `GET /api/feature-settings`, which the proxy 404s on the public zone, spinning the old retry every 15s forever on a customer's phone; `/privacy` is now a customer path, so no staff provider or nav runs there. **(4)** [CheckoutModal](src/components/pos/CheckoutModal.js)'s table-load moved out of the `[isOpen]`-only reset effect into its own effect keyed on the *loaded* mode, so opening checkout before settings arrive no longer fires a wasted `/api/tables` fetch + floor-plan flash in a typed shop, and a mode flip while the modal is open now re-loads instead of leaving no table picker at all. Also: the staff-surface predicate (`!isCustomerPath && !== '/login'`), previously hand-copied in five files, is now one `isStaffPath()` helper in [staffPaths.js](src/lib/staffPaths.js); the admin nav links are `useMemo`'d instead of rebuilt every render; and the context no longer exposes an unused `refresh`. Build clean, 51/51 tests pass. No money, payment, auth, or zone logic touched.
- **`public/sw.js` could go missing on the production service, silently disabling the offline app-shell.** The previous commit made `public/sw.js` a generated, gitignored file produced only by `npm run dev`/`build` — but the NSSM production service runs `npm run start` (no generator), and its installer preflight checks only `.next`, so pulling that commit (which deletes the now-untracked file) and restarting without a rebuild left `/sw.js` 404ing, swallowed silently by `ServiceWorkerRegister`'s `.catch`. Fixes: a **`prestart`** hook ([package.json](package.json)) regenerates the worker before `next start`, so every serve mode has it; [scripts/bump-sw-cache.js](scripts/bump-sw-cache.js) now keys `CACHE_VERSION` on the git commit id **without** a per-run timestamp, so regenerating on each start/dev is byte-stable (no more forced tablet reload on every restart, and `git status` no longer dirties on dev) and only busts the cache on a real deploy; the generator uses `split`/`join` rather than `String.replace` (a `$` in the version can't trigger replacement patterns) and hard-fails if the template's placeholder count isn't exactly one; [verify-resilience.ps1](scripts/verify-resilience.ps1) guards the missing file with a clear message instead of a raw PowerShell error; and [AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md), the Cursor rules, and the go-live checklist no longer describe `sw.js` as hand-written/committed. Ops + docs only — no app runtime logic changed.

### Changed
- **Feature settings now load once through a shared `FeatureSettingsContext` instead of five components each fetching `/api/feature-settings` on their own — and the settings-toggle "nudge" via the auth event is gone.** Closes the four remaining code-review findings from the typed-table-numbers review in one move. `manualTableNumbers` (and friends) were fetched independently by [BottomNav](src/components/shared/BottomNav.js), [CheckoutModal](src/components/pos/CheckoutModal.js), the [admin layout](src/app/admin/layout.js), the [manager menu page](src/app/manager/menu/page.js), and the [settings page](src/app/admin/settings/page.js) — five HTTP requests and Prisma round trips for the same `FeatureSettings id=1` row, five private copies, and each needing its own trick to stay fresh. That last need is what had BottomNav listening on **`session-changed`** — an *authentication* event fired from six places (logins, logouts, admin/manager PIN entry, the refund re-auth) — so every PIN entry re-fetched a toggle that hadn't changed, and the live Tables-tab update depended entirely on the settings page continuing to fire an auth event after a PATCH ("nudge it"). New [src/contexts/FeatureSettingsContext.js](src/contexts/FeatureSettingsContext.js) (same pattern as `OnlineOrdersContext`, mounted in the root layout, inactive on customer paths so the public zone sees zero new requests) is now the **only** fetcher: it checks `res.ok` (the route's 500 body is valid JSON — the bug fixed below), keeps the last-known flags on failure with a 15s retry instead of guessing a default, uses an `AbortController` so overlapping refreshes can't land out of order and unmount aborts the request in flight (the review's race finding), and fetches with `cache: 'no-store'` — resolving the review's inconsistency finding (admin layout and manager menu said `no-store`, BottomNav and CheckoutModal didn't) by there being exactly one fetch to decide for. After a settings PATCH the settings page pushes the response through `apply()`, so the nav, checkout, and admin links update **immediately and by design** rather than as a side effect of an auth event — the `session-changed` dispatch in the settings page is deleted (its six auth-related dispatch/listen sites are untouched; they really are about sessions). Consumers shrank accordingly: BottomNav's whole fetch-and-listen effect became one context read, CheckoutModal derives `manualTables`/`tableNumberMax` instead of re-fetching on every modal open, and the admin layout derives its Expenses/Purchasing/Vouchers links instead of re-reading flags on every session check. One deliberate behaviour change: the settings page no longer paints made-up defaults when the initial read fails — it keeps "Loading…" (with the context retrying) rather than showing an admin every toggle as "off" and inviting them to "fix" state that isn't real. Build clean, 51/51 tests pass. No money, payment, auth, or zone logic touched.
- **`public/sw.js` is no longer a committed file that every build rewrites — the source moved to [scripts/sw.template.js](scripts/sw.template.js) and the built file is generated + gitignored.** The last review finding: `CACHE_VERSION` is stamped from `git rev-parse --short HEAD` + a timestamp on every build, so the committed copy was permanently one commit behind reality, `git status` was dirty after every build, and every branch conflicted on that one line (the history is full of `fix(service-worker): update cache version` commits doing nothing else). [scripts/bump-sw-cache.js](scripts/bump-sw-cache.js) now reads the template, fills in `__CACHE_VERSION__`, and writes `public/sw.js`; the generation runs on **both** `npm run build` and `npm run dev` (so a fresh clone serves `/sw.js` in dev too), and `/public/sw.js` is untracked + gitignored. The worker's logic is byte-identical — same app-shell caching, same `/api/` and navigation passthroughs — only where the source of truth lives changed.

### Added
- **Typed table numbers — the cashier types the number of the stand handed to the customer, instead of picking a pre-defined table off the floor plan. Toggleable in Admin → Settings.** How the shop actually works: the customer takes a numbered stand at the counter and sits wherever they like, so the number is a *token the customer carries*, not a fixed spot on a map — the server walks the floor looking for that number. The POS only supported the opposite model (pick a `Table` row off the saved layout), and its only alternative was a free-text **Customer name** field, so a table number could only be recorded by typing it in as if it were a person's name. New **Table Numbers** card in [src/app/admin/settings/page.js](src/app/admin/settings/page.js) switches between **Typed number** and **Floor plan**, backed by `FeatureSettings.manualTableNumbers` (default **false**, so an untouched shop keeps the floor plan exactly as-is) plus `tableNumberMax` (default 20) driving how many quick-pick buttons checkout offers — set it to how many stands the shop hands out. In typed mode [CheckoutModal](src/components/pos/CheckoutModal.js) shows a numeric field + a quick-pick button per number (tap 12 → done; typing is there for anything past the buttons), skips the `/api/tables` fetch entirely (no floor plan behind it), and the **Tables** tab drops out of [BottomNav](src/components/shared/BottomNav.js) since a typed number has no `Table` row to mark occupied — and the tab appears/disappears **live** when the mode is toggled: BottomNav reads the setting once on mount and would otherwise stay stale until a full reload (switching back to Floor plan left the Tables tab hidden), so it now also re-reads on the `session-changed` event the Settings toggle already fires, matching how NavBar and the admin/manager layouts react to session changes. Table number and customer name stay **mutually exclusive** — they're two ways of naming the same order, so setting either clears the other, exactly as the table grid already behaved. **The mode is decided server-side, never by the client**: `POST /api/orders` ([src/app/api/orders/route.js](src/app/api/orders/route.js)) re-reads the setting and nulls any `tableId` a stale POS tab sends in typed mode (so an order can never occupy a table the cashier didn't pick), and refuses a client-claimed `tableManual` when the shop is in floor-plan mode. New `Order.tableManual` (Boolean, default false) exists because `tableNumber` is **overloaded** — with a `tableId` it's a table, without one it's the customer's name — and a typed number has no `tableId`, so without a marker "12" would print and display as a *customer called 12*; the flag lets `orderLocationLabel` ([src/lib/formatters.js](src/lib/formatters.js)) and the order-detail modal tell the two apart. Existing rows default false, so every historical order keeps the label it has today. **Verified by driving the real app in Chrome, which caught two bugs unit tests would not have**: **(1)** [src/app/pos/page.js](src/app/pos/page.js) rebuilds the POST body field-by-field and silently **dropped `tableManual`** — the sale saved `tableManual=false` and the docket would have read a bare "12"; **(2)** `listFailedDockets` ([src/lib/printQueue.js](src/lib/printQueue.js)) `select`ed `tableNumber` but not `tableManual`, so the reprint queue showed "12" instead of "Table 12" (`getPrintJob` uses `include`, so the reprinted docket itself was never affected). After both fixes, a real cash sale through the till posted `tableId:null, tableNumber:"12", tableManual:true` → **201**, and the Print Queue rendered **"#155 · Table 12"** — the docket line the server reads. Probed: quick-pick count follows the setting (max 6 → 6 buttons); letters/symbols are stripped (`A7b!` → `7`) and entry caps at 3 digits; name/number exclusivity holds both ways; `tableNumberMax` rejects 0/100/−5/`"abc"` with a 400 and accepts 1–99; a stale `tableId:2` in typed mode saved `null` and left table 2 **AVAILABLE**; a client claiming `tableManual` in floor-plan mode saved **false**. **Floor-plan mode regression-tested end-to-end**: toggled back, the Tables tab returns, the T1–T6 grid renders with no typed field, and a sale saved `tableId=1` and marked Table 1 **OCCUPIED**. 51/51 tests pass. No money math, payment capture, auth, or zone logic touched.

### Fixed
- **A failed feature-settings read silently switched the nav back to floor-plan mode, putting the Tables tab back in a typed-number shop.** [BottomNav](src/components/shared/BottomNav.js) read `/api/feature-settings` without checking `res.ok`, and the route's own error path ([src/app/api/feature-settings/route.js](src/app/api/feature-settings/route.js)) returns **`{ error: … }` with a 500** — which is *valid JSON*. So `r.json()` resolved, the `.catch` never ran, `d?.manualTableNumbers` came back `undefined`, and `!!undefined` wrote **`false`** = floor plan. The `.catch` had the same flaw from the other direction: it *also* forced `false`, so a network blip picked a mode rather than leaving the current one alone. Both paths now fail closed on the **state**, not on a guessed default: a non-OK response throws, and a failed read leaves `manualTables` untouched (on mount it stays at the `false` initial state — the documented default — so first-load behaviour is unchanged). This mattered more after the live-toggle change above bound the read to `session-changed`: what was a one-shot risk at mount became reachable on **any** staff login or manager PIN entry, so a transient DB hiccup mid-shift could flip a typed-number shop's nav back to floor plan and offer a `/tables` page that mode has no use for. Found by code review of the live-toggle commit. Build clean, 51/51 tests pass. No money, payment, auth, or zone logic touched.
- **Refunding after the manager session quietly expired dead-ended on a cryptic "Forbidden" with no way forward.** The refund route requires a valid `manager_session` (1-hour TTL), but the Manager layout only checks the session on mount, and the Sales list API is unauthenticated — so once the 1h token lapsed, the page kept rendering fine and the *first* thing to enforce manager auth was the refund POST itself, which returned a bare **403 "Forbidden"** ([src/components/admin/sales/RefundModal.js](src/components/admin/sales/RefundModal.js) surfaced that raw string). Staff had no hint that the fix was simply "re-enter your PIN." Now a 403 from the refund call **(1)** shows a plain-language message — *"Your manager session expired — please re-enter your PIN"* — instead of "Forbidden", and **(2)** pops the existing `ManagerPinModal` in place; on a successful PIN the refund is **retried automatically** (and a `session-changed` event re-syncs the Manager layout), turning a confusing dead-end into a one-tap recovery. UX only — no change to the refund money logic, the auth rules, or the 1h session policy; the server still returns 403 and still requires MANAGER/ADMIN. Build clean.

### Fixed
- **Two maintenance scheduled tasks that the docs claimed were registered had, in fact, never been registered — and one of the two registration scripts could not have worked.** A log-audit of the 24/7 box found only **DreamyCafe DB Backup** in Task Scheduler; **DreamyCafe DB Maintenance** and **DreamyCafe Restore Drill** were absent, despite [docs/database-backup.md](docs/database-backup.md) stating maintenance *"is registered to run monthly."* Consequence on an always-on machine: the monthly `VACUUM (ANALYZE)` + `PrintJob` prune never ran (Postgres slowly bloats/deplans over a year of trading) and `desktop.log` was never trimmed (nothing else bounds it — [scripts/db-maintenance.ps1](scripts/db-maintenance.ps1) does both, but only if scheduled), and the quarterly `pg_restore` drill that proves backups actually restore never ran. Root cause for the drill: [scripts/register-restore-drill-task.ps1](scripts/register-restore-drill-task.ps1) was UTF-8 **without a BOM** and contained an em-dash in its final `Write-Output`; Windows PowerShell 5.1 reads BOM-less files as Windows-1252, corrupting the em-dash into a broken string literal (*"missing the terminator"*) — a **parse error**, so the script aborted before `Register-ScheduledTask` ever ran, which is why the task was perpetually missing. Fix: replaced the em-dash with a plain hyphen (matching the working maintenance script), and registered both tasks (elevated) — Task Scheduler now shows all three DreamyCafe jobs `Ready` (Maintenance next 19/07 03:00, Restore Drill next 20/07 09:00). Also closed the same **silent-failure logging gap** in the refund path that was just closed on the order path: `POST /api/orders/[id]/refund` ([src/app/api/orders/[id]/refund/route.js](src/app/api/orders/[id]/refund/route.js)) returned *"Refund failed"* to staff but logged **nothing** server-side — a real money event (processor refused to return the customer's money) left no trace in `desktop.log`; both the processor-refusal branch and the outer catch now `console.error`. Ops + logging only — no money math, payment capture, auth, or zone logic touched.

### Added
- **Card sales now auto-retry the *save* (never the charge) when it fails, and staff can watch it happen.** Prompted by the owner noticing that the fast-food POS they also work on says *"Try Again"* when a tap doesn't register. That pattern is right — but only for the failure where **no money moved** (declined / timed out / cancelled), which this app already covers via the terminal overlay's existing **Try again** button. The "Sale not saved — the card may have already been charged" dialog is the *opposite* case: the tap **did** register, the money **has** moved, and re-tapping would **double-charge**. The safe retry there is re-sending the *order*, not re-taking the *payment* — and `POST /api/orders` was **already built to make that safe** ([src/app/api/orders/route.js](src/app/api/orders/route.js) dedupes on `paymentIntentId`, returning the already-recorded order, with a `P2002` guard for the race) — nothing was using it. New [src/lib/saveOrder.js](src/lib/saveOrder.js) `postOrderWithRetry()` retries the save up to 3 times, 1s apart, on failures a retry can actually fix: a response that never arrived, a **402** (the card-verify losing its race with Square's capture — see the fix below), or a **5xx**. A 400-class rejection is a bad payload and is **not** retried. **Critical safety boundary: only payloads carrying a `paymentIntentId` are ever retried.** A cash sale has no idempotency key, so retrying a merely-lost response would ring the sale **twice** — cash is therefore never retried, and on failure still stops and asks a human. Both save paths in [src/app/pos/page.js](src/app/pos/page.js) (cart checkout **and** Quick Sale, including split-payment) now share one `saveOrderVisibly()` helper instead of two divergent copies of the failure logic. Per the owner's explicit request the recovery is **visible, not silent**: a fixed banner (above the modals) reads *"Payment taken — saving sale… (try 2 of 3)"* during retries, and a sale that recovers reports *"…(saved on retry — charged once)"* for 8s rather than pretending nothing happened. If every auto-retry fails, a **card** sale now gets a *"Try saving again"* / *"Stop"* choice — the charge already happened, so saving again is the safe action and re-ringing is not — instead of only "OK" and a manual refund. Also fixes a genuine UX lie found while wiring this: after the tap succeeded, the modal overlay still read **"Tap or insert card — Waiting for $X on the reader"** while the order saved; both [CheckoutModal](src/components/pos/CheckoutModal.js) and [QuickSaleModal](src/components/pos/QuickSaleModal.js) now show **"Payment approved — saving the sale, don't charge again."** **Verified**: retry logic 9/9 (`402→200` recovers in 2 calls — the real bug; network-error and `5xx` recover; `402×3` gives up after exactly 3; `400` never retried; **cash never retried on `5xx` or network error**), and the `recovered` flag drives the staff message correctly. Idempotency proven **against the live DB, not assumed**: a seeded order re-POSTed with the same `paymentIntentId` returned **HTTP 200 with the same order id** and left **exactly one** order for that ref. The new 402 logging confirmed firing live (`[order] card verify FAILED for pay:… (provider SQUARE): Status code: 404`) — a failure that would previously have left no trace at all. 51/51 tests pass, build clean. **Now exercised by a real tap on the reader** (see the hardware confirmation on the `APPROVED` fix below).

### Fixed
- **Intermittent "Sale not saved — the card may have already been charged" on in-person card sales: a real payment was being rejected as unpaid.** Square exposes **two objects with two independent statuses**, and the Square adapter conflated them. Per [Square's Terminal API docs](https://developer.squareup.com/docs/terminal-api/square-terminal-payments), when the buyer taps, *"the Terminal checkout transitions to COMPLETED and includes the resulting payment_id. **The Payment object now has a status of APPROVED**"* — i.e. `COMPLETED` on the **checkout** does not mean `COMPLETED` on the **payment**; `APPROVED` means the card is authorized and autocapture (default `autocomplete: true`, which this app relies on) settles it to `COMPLETED` moments later. But `squareProvider.verify()` in [src/lib/paymentProvider.js](src/lib/paymentProvider.js) accepted **only** `COMPLETED`, so `POST /api/orders` ([src/app/api/orders/route.js](src/app/api/orders/route.js)) rejected the order with a 402 whenever the POS saved the order before Square finished capturing — a **race**, which is why it only happened *sometimes* on identical taps (and, perversely, more often on a *faster* LAN/server). Failure mode was the worst kind: **card charged, no order recorded**, leaving staff to spot it and refund by hand. The decisive evidence was an **asymmetry between the two adapters in the same file**: `stripeProvider.verify()` already accepts `requires_capture` — Stripe's exact equivalent of Square's `APPROVED` (authorized, not yet captured) — alongside `succeeded`, so the Square path was the outlier, not the rule. Fix: accept `APPROVED` **and** `COMPLETED` as paid, restoring parity with the Stripe adapter. Deliberately **no retry/backoff** — `APPROVED` *is* the expected state at this instant, so polling for `COMPLETED` would add latency to every successful card sale to wait for something already accepted as paid (and Stripe doesn't retry either). Residual risk is small and strictly better than the status quo: an order can be saved a beat before capture finalises, but `charge()` never sets `autocomplete: false`, so `APPROVED` is purely transitional — versus today's regular, real "charged with no order". **Scope deliberately minimal**: the *online* Square paths ([src/lib/onlineCheckout.js](src/lib/onlineCheckout.js), [src/app/api/webhooks/square/route.js](src/app/api/webhooks/square/route.js)) still require `COMPLETED` and are **correct as-is** — they're not one-shot, they self-heal (`payment.updated` fires again on the transition to `COMPLETED`, and the success page + stale-order sweep re-poll), so an `APPROVED` reading there is simply retried; the in-person terminal check was the only **one-shot** verify, which is why this only ever bit card sales at the till. Also added the missing **server-side logging** on both 402 branches (`verify` returning false, and `verify` throwing): the rejection path previously logged nothing, so these failures left **no trace in `logs/desktop.log`** — confirmed by grepping the log and finding none — making a charged-but-unsaved sale invisible after the fact. **Verified**: the shipped `verify()` exercised against every Square payment status — `COMPLETED`→paid, `APPROVED`→paid (the fix), `PENDING`/`CANCELED`/`FAILED`/missing-payment/malformed-ref→**not** paid (7/7); 51/51 tests pass. **Now confirmed on the paired reader**: a $1 test run with temporary status logging captured *both* states across two taps — `status=APPROVED -> paid` (the exact race this fix addresses; the old `COMPLETED`-only check would have 402'd it, charging the card with no order) and `status=COMPLETED -> paid` — proving the reader genuinely returns `APPROVED` at check-time and that the app now rings the sale in that case. Temporary log line removed afterward (it doesn't belong in the payment path).

### Added
- **Per-day online ordering hours + an "Accept Online Orders" master switch, configurable in Admin → Settings.** The Cloudflare tunnel runs 24/7 but the shop doesn't, and the app had **no concept of opening hours at all** — no schema field, no gate on the public order path — so a customer could pay for a coffee at 3am and the order would be taken with nobody there to make it. Hours are **per weekday** (`OnlineOrderHours`, one row per `dayOfWeek` 0–6 with its own `openTime`/`closeTime` and a `closed` flag), because the shop trades different hours on weekdays vs weekends; a day being closed is just that day set `closed`, so there's one concept instead of a separate closed-days list. A singleton `OnlineOrderSettings` (`hoursEnabled`, `acceptingOrders`, `closedMessage`) holds the week-wide switches, following the existing `FeatureSettings`/`ShiftSettings` pattern. The open/closed decision lives in one place — `evaluateOpen()` in [src/lib/onlineOrderSettings.js](src/lib/onlineOrderSettings.js) — so the API, the gate and the UI can't disagree. **The authoritative gate is server-side**: `POST /api/orders/online` ([src/app/api/orders/online/route.js](src/app/api/orders/online/route.js)) refuses with **409** *before* any order row, stock hold, or Square payment link is created — per the repo rule that the public zone never trusts the client, since hiding a button is not a closed shop. Closed copy names the **real** next opening rather than repeating today's time (`nextOpening()` scans the week, so Friday 8pm correctly reads *"open tomorrow at 8am"*, and a fully-closed week yields no invented time). The customer page ([src/components/public/PublicOnlineOrderPage.js](src/components/public/PublicOnlineOrderPage.js)) shows the closed banner, disables the Pay button, and still lets people browse the menu; it reads state from the **already-whitelisted** `/api/payment-settings/public` (extended rather than adding a new public route, so [src/proxy.js](src/proxy.js) needed **no** change). Admin gets a per-day editor (Open/Closed switch + open/close pickers per day, "copy Monday → weekdays" / "copy Saturday → Sunday" shortcuts, and an explicit Save so a half-typed time never hits the DB) plus a live "currently open/closed" indicator ([src/app/admin/settings/page.js](src/app/admin/settings/page.js)); writes are `verifyAdminAccess`-gated, validate `HH:MM` + day numbers server-side, reject duplicate days, and upsert the week in **one transaction** so a partial write can't leave a half-saved week. Times are plain shop-local comparisons — correct because the server clock is already pinned to the shop timezone (`TZ=Australia/Sydney` via NSSM / `APP_TZ`, see DEPLOY.md) — and a `closeTime <= openTime` window wraps past midnight, including spilling into the following day (Fri 18:00–02:00 leaves Sat 01:00 open). Settings **fail open**: an unmigrated table or a read error leaves ordering enabled, so a missing migration can never block a paying customer. **Verified end-to-end against the running app**: 19/19 logic cases pass — notably *Sat 17:00 → closed*, proving weekday hours don't leak onto weekend days — plus the boundaries (06:59 closed / 07:00 open / 17:59 open / 18:00 closed), closed-days, pause-overrides-hours, and both midnight-wrap cases; against the live API a **fully valid order payload** while closed returned `409 {"closed":true,"reason":"OUTSIDE_HOURS"}` with *"open tomorrow at 7am"*, an all-day closure returned `reason:"CLOSED_DAY"`, and **0 order rows** were created by the blocked attempts, while the same request passed the gate once hours were restored. 51/51 tests pass, build clean. No money math, payment capture, auth, or zone logic touched.

- **"Quick Sale" custom-amount keypad in the POS — type a dollar amount and charge it straight to the Square Terminal (or record as cash), bypassing the cart.** Requested because the owner's previous Lightspeed reader had an integrated keypad + POS on the device; the Square Terminal in Terminal-API mode is a customer-facing reader driven by the app, so it has no standalone keypad. Rather than switch the reader into Square's own POS (which would leave sales invisible to DreamyCafe), this puts the keypad *in* DreamyCafe so custom sales still record as normal orders. New [src/components/pos/QuickSaleModal.js](src/components/pos/QuickSaleModal.js): a numpad (digits + `.` + backspace) that accumulates the amount as an exact string, with an optional note and a cashier selector; **Charge to card** reuses the existing `runTerminalCharge` charge→poll→finalize helper and the same processing/failed overlay + error copy as CheckoutModal, and **Cash** records with `change: 0`. Opened from a new **Quick Sale** button in the POS toolbar ([src/app/pos/page.js](src/app/pos/page.js)), gated on the till being open. The sale is saved via a new cart-independent `handleQuickSale` that POSTs a single synthetic `{ productId: null, productName: 'Custom amount', unitPrice: <amount>, notes: <note> }` line to `POST /api/orders` — mirroring the existing order-save **failure safety verbatim** (on failure the modal stays open and, for card, warns the card may already have been charged, so a charge is never left with no order). **No changes to any API route, payment provider, or schema** — the terminal charge pipeline is amount-driven and provider-agnostic, and `OrderItem.productId` is already nullable. Amount handling goes through `src/lib/money.js` (`D`/`gt`), never a float. The card path inherits the shared charge flow's `skipReceiptScreen` behaviour.

### Fixed
- **Paid online orders showed "Amount Paid $0.00" on the order detail/receipt even though the card was charged in full.** Online orders are created with `amountPaid`/`change` = 0 while `AWAITING_PAYMENT` (correct — no money has moved yet), but `fulfillPaidOrder` ([src/lib/fulfillment.js](src/lib/fulfillment.js)) never backfilled `amountPaid` once the Square/Stripe payment confirmed, so a fully-paid online order read "Amount Paid $0.00 / Change $0.00" — looking, alarmingly, like it hadn't been paid (an in-person card tap correctly records `amountPaid = total`). The money was always collected and the order correctly marked Paid/`COMPLETED`; only this display field was stale. Fix: when the atomic status-flip promotes the order `AWAITING_PAYMENT → PENDING`, also set `amountPaid = total` and `change = 0` (exact-charge card payment, no change), so online orders now read "$X paid" like a reader tap. Read the order's `total` first (Prisma `updateMany` can't copy a row's own column into another), then stamp it inside the same conditional `where` claim — exactly-once semantics unchanged (a raced second caller still flips 0 rows and no-ops). Confirmed no report/reconciliation sums `amountPaid` (revenue is computed from `order.total`), so this is display-only — no money rollup affected. Verified: 51/51 tests pass, build clean. Side benefit: the receipt's "Paid" line (suppressed when `amountPaid` is 0) now prints for online orders too.
- **Paid online orders were stuck in Square's dashboard "Active" tab because the auto-complete `orders.update` was rejected.** `completeSquareOnlineFulfillment` (in [src/lib/square.js](src/lib/square.js)) fetches the paid Square order and marks its fulfillment `COMPLETED` so the order leaves "Active" — but the update was failing, leaving the order Paid-but-Active in the dashboard (the DB order was always correctly marked Paid/fulfilled). Three fixes, each confirmed from `logs/desktop.log`: **(1)** the `order` body omitted `locationId`, which Square's Orders API requires (`Missing required key "locationId"`) — now passes the fetched order's `locationId`; **(2)** the Payment Link order already carries a `DIGITAL`/`PROPOSED` fulfillment, so complete *that* existing fulfillment by `uid` (`{ uid, state: 'COMPLETED' }`) rather than bolting on a second `PICKUP` (falls back to `PICKUP` only if no existing fulfillment/uid); **(3)** `VERSION_MISMATCH` — Square bumps the order's version as the payment/DIGITAL fulfillment settle, so a version read once was stale by update time (`Supplied version 6 does not match the current version 7`) — wrapped the fetch+update in a retry loop (up to 4 attempts, re-fetching the current version each time with a short escalating backoff), retrying only on `VERSION_MISMATCH`. Cosmetic/dashboard-only path (guarded, never throws, never blocks the webhook ack or order fulfillment) — no money math, payment capture, auth, or zone logic touched.

### Changed
- **Skip the Square Terminal "How would you like your receipt?" screen after every reader payment.** The POS drives the paired Square Terminal (1st Gen, v2) server-side via the Terminal Checkout API, so after each card charge the reader was showing Square's buyer-facing receipt-selection screen (Email / Text / No receipt), forcing an extra tap per sale. The on-device toggle for this (**Settings → Checkout → Signature and receipt → Skip receipt screen**) doesn't exist in the standalone Terminal's menu — that path only lives in the Square iPad/phone app, and Square's own community forum documents the on-device skip as unreliable for card payments anyway. The reliable fix is API-side: set `skipReceiptScreen: true` in the `deviceOptions` of the `terminal.checkouts.create` request in [src/lib/paymentProvider.js](src/lib/paymentProvider.js) (Square field `device_options.skip_receipt_screen`, confirmed against the pinned Square API version `2026-05-20`). One-field change on the charge path; no amount, currency, deadline, or status-mapping logic touched.

### Security
- **Pre-publish hardening: removed the hardcoded Postgres password from the ops scripts and scrubbed personal paths from docs, ahead of making the repo public.** A fresh full-history + working-tree audit reconfirmed the important things are clean — `.env` **never** committed, no live Stripe/Square/cloud secret values in tracked code — but two things would have shipped to a public repo: **(1)** the real DB password `1234` was **hardcoded in three scripts** ([scripts/backup-db.ps1](scripts/backup-db.ps1), [scripts/db-maintenance.ps1](scripts/db-maintenance.ps1), [scripts/restore-drill.ps1](scripts/restore-drill.ps1) — the first grep missed it because the value is only 4 chars). Replaced each with a `Get-DbPassword` helper that reads `PGPASSWORD` from the environment/`.env`, else parses it out of `DATABASE_URL` (single source of truth), and added a fail-fast guard if none resolves. Also de-hardcoded the PostgreSQL bin path (`PG_BIN` override) and the backup dir (derived from the script's own location via `$PSScriptRoot`, `BACKUP_DIR` override) so the scripts carry no machine-specific paths, and collapsed a duplicated `.env`-reader into the shared helper. **Verified end-to-end**: `backup-db.ps1` runs green — resolves the password from `DATABASE_URL`, writes + integrity-verifies a real dump, and mirrors to Drive. **(2)** Replaced the personal `C:\Users\John\…` paths with generic placeholders (`C:\path\to\DreamyCafe`, `C:\Users\YourName`) across AGENTS.md, RUNBOOK.md, the `docs/` guides, and the Cursor rules file (BOM-free, diff limited to the changed lines). Deliberately **kept** the real `dreamy-cafe.com` domain references — behind the Cloudflare Tunnel the public only ever sees the customer flow, and a working domain is proof the app runs. No app/money/auth/zone code changed — scripts + docs only.

### Fixed
- **Demo seed usability + a documented UTC timezone gotcha, surfaced while validating the live Vercel demo.** Three things, all demo-scoped (no shop code path affected): **(1) Seeded Staff had no PIN** — Admin/Manager got a hashed `pin` but Staff didn't, so it appeared in the POS "Switch User" list yet `POST /api/auth/verify-pin` rejected every attempt with *"No PIN set for this account"* (`if (!user.pin)`), making the staff till login impossible on a fresh seed. Gave the seeded Staff a PIN (`1111`, distinct from Manager `1234` / Owner `0000`) in [prisma/seedData.js](prisma/seedData.js) and surfaced it in [src/components/shared/DemoBanner.js](src/components/shared/DemoBanner.js). **(2) Demo till started with nobody clocked in** — "Switch User" only lists users whose most-recent clock record today is `IN`, so a visitor hit an empty list and couldn't ring a sale until discovering the In/Out flow (and the nightly reseed reset it to empty each night). `seedInto` now creates an `IN` clock record for Staff **only when `NEXT_PUBLIC_DEMO === 'true'`** (real shops must reflect real shifts), so the demo always lands on a ready till after each reset. Verified both against the live Neon DB: `bcrypt.compare('1111', …)` passes and the real `clockedIn=1` filter now returns `['Staff']`. **(3) Documented the UTC day-boundary gotcha** — cash orders rang on the live demo didn't appear in Reports/Order History because Vercel runs Node in **UTC**: `parseReportDateRange`/`startOfLocalDay` build the day window from the *server's* local timezone while the browser's date picker uses the *viewer's* local date, so on a UTC host "today" is a different calendar day and same-day orders fall outside the queried window (confirmed: two `COMPLETED` `$7.50` CASH orders saved fine at `…T14:57Z/14:59Z`, i.e. the 9th in AEST but the 8th in UTC — the write path is correct, only the read window was off). Fix: since the standard `TZ` env var is **reserved on Vercel/Lambda** (can't be set in the dashboard), added [src/instrumentation.js](src/instrumentation.js) (Next's once-at-startup hook) that applies a non-reserved `APP_TZ` to `process.env.TZ` before the app serves requests — so `new Date('…T00:00:00')`/`setHours`/`getHours` reason in the shop timezone. Set `APP_TZ=Australia/Sydney` on the demo; unset on the LAN shop (OS local time is already correct → no-op). Verified the day window now includes the `14:59Z` order and the production build compiles the instrumentation. Documented `APP_TZ` (and the `TZ`-is-reserved reason) in [docs/demo-deploy.md](docs/demo-deploy.md). No money math, auth, or zone logic changed.

### Added
- **Cloud portfolio-demo scaffolding — a public, all-access, nightly-resetting demo of the app on Vercel + Neon, without touching any production/shop code path.** For job-application use: a live URL a recruiter can click into and explore POS/Manager/Owner. Deliberately the *inverse* of the real deployment — no LAN zone lockdown (`PUBLIC_ZONE_SECRET` left **unset** so the proxy treats every route as trusted and the whole app is reachable, which is what a demo wants), no payment keys (card checkout simply unavailable so nothing can attempt a real charge — the **cash** path exercises the full order flow with no hardware), printers no-op (already non-fatal). Pieces: **(1)** extracted the canonical seed body into [prisma/seedData.js](prisma/seedData.js) (`seedInto(prisma)`) so the CLI seed and the cloud reseed write an identical shop; [prisma/seed.js](prisma/seed.js) now just calls it (behaviour unchanged — still upserts the same 1 admin / 1 manager / 1 staff / 4 categories / 15 products / 6 tables / 2 printer configs). **(2)** [src/lib/demoReseed.js](src/lib/demoReseed.js) `reseedDemo()` — dynamically discovers every `public` table from `pg_tables` (excluding `_prisma_migrations`) and `TRUNCATE … RESTART IDENTITY CASCADE`s them, then reseeds; table discovery is dynamic so a future migration can't leave stale demo rows behind. **Hard-guarded**: throws unless `NEXT_PUBLIC_DEMO === 'true'`, so this destructive op can never fire against a real shop DB. **(3)** [src/app/api/demo/reseed/route.js](src/app/api/demo/reseed/route.js) — the nightly entry point, gated by a `CRON_SECRET` Bearer token (Vercel Cron signs with it) **and** the `NEXT_PUBLIC_DEMO` guard (defence in depth). **(4)** [vercel.json](vercel.json) registers a Vercel Cron on `/api/demo/reseed` at `0 15 * * *` (15:00 UTC = 01:00 AEST, shop-quiet). **(5)** [src/components/shared/DemoBanner.js](src/components/shared/DemoBanner.js) — a dismissible fixed-position corner pill (only rendered when `NEXT_PUBLIC_DEMO==='true'`) telling visitors it's a demo, that data resets nightly, that card/printing are off, and the login PINs (Manager `1234`, Owner `0000`); mounted in [src/app/layout.js](src/app/layout.js). Styled in the repo's neutral black/white/gray design system (no amber). **(6)** a standalone `node prisma/seed-demo.js` CLI (`npm run db:seed:demo`) for the one-time / on-demand reseed against Neon, and [docs/demo-deploy.md](docs/demo-deploy.md) — full Vercel+Neon deploy guide (Neon pooled string, env-var table, first-time `migrate deploy` + seed, verify steps, and the by-design demo limitations: serverless read-only FS breaks product-image *upload* — seeded emoji images are fine — card/terminal off, printing no-op). No money/payment/auth/zone logic changed; the demo relies on the *existing* cash path and the *existing* unset-secret zone default.

### Changed
- **Restructured `README.md` to lead with the engineering story for portfolio/hiring use, while preserving the full operator guide below.** The old README was a strong *operator* doc (setup, `.env`, run scripts) but buried what makes the project portfolio-worthy under boilerplate a hiring manager won't read. New top-of-file leads with a hero blurb, a stack/scale one-liner, and a **"Why this project is worth a look"** section framing the four decisions that carry the engineering weight: (1) **money is never a float** — `decimal.js` via [src/lib/money.js](src/lib/money.js), no raw operators on money, Decimal(10,2) storage, string-over-the-wire, integer minor units to processors; (2) the **LAN/public zone trust boundary** — Cloudflare-Tunnel `x-dreamy-zone` header validated in [src/lib/zone.js](src/lib/zone.js), allowlist in [src/proxy.js](src/proxy.js), and server-side reprice of every online `unitPrice` from ids/qty/size only; (3) **pluggable Stripe/Square providers** with signed, idempotent webhooks and server-driven smart readers; (4) **graceful offline degradation** (cash still works when only the WAN is down, hand-written auto-versioned service worker). Added an ASCII architecture diagram, a **Screenshots** placeholder section (recruiters skim visuals first), a `## Roles` table, and a live-demo callout for the coming seeded instance. All original operator content — prerequisites, `.env` template, run scripts, npm/dev-test script tables, deployment, docs index, license — is retained verbatim below the story. Verified every technical claim against the code before shipping it: migration count (**46** → stated "45+"), the seven `test/*.test.js` files, and the existence of `toCents` + the `x-dreamy-zone` header name. Docs-only — no app code touched. Part of the pre-publish portfolio prep alongside the repo-hygiene cleanup below.

### Removed
- **Untracked a stray SQLite database (`prisma/prisma/dev.db`) that had slipped past `.gitignore`, ahead of making the repo public.** A pre-publish audit of every tracked file and the full 186-commit history came back clean on the things that matter — `.env` was **never** committed, and there are **no** live secret values anywhere in tracked code (`sk_live`/`whsec_`/Square `EAAA…`/AWS keys/private keys) — only `sk_test` string-prefix *checks* in terminal logic. The one real finding was `prisma/prisma/dev.db`, committed once early (`13ce740`) and tracked ever since: the `.gitignore` rule `prisma/*.db` only covers the top level, so the **nested** `prisma/prisma/` path escaped it. Contents were verified to be **seed data only** (15 demo products — Espresso/Latte/… — 4 categories, 2 test orders; no customer emails/passwords/tokens), so no history rewrite (BFG/filter-repo) is needed — a plain `git rm --cached` suffices and the file stays on disk for local dev. Tightened `.gitignore` with `prisma/**/*.db` + `prisma/**/*.db-journal` so any nested db can never be committed again (verified via `git check-ignore`). Repo-hygiene only — no app code touched.

### Added
- **[docs/migrate-to-new-pc.md](docs/migrate-to-new-pc.md) — ordered procedure for cloning a live shop onto a fresh 24/7 mini PC.** The existing docs cover the *first* install ([DEPLOY.md](DEPLOY.md), [go-live-checklist.md](docs/go-live-checklist.md)) and backup/restore ([database-backup.md](docs/database-backup.md)), but never the distinct **migration** case — moving real data, reusing secrets, and cutting the Cloudflare tunnel over without two machines contending. The doc splits the work into **zero-downtime prep on the new PC while the old one still trades** (hardware/timezone/UPS, prerequisites matching the old PC's PostgreSQL major version, `git clone` + `npm install`, copy the un-backed-up `.env`, empty DB + `prisma migrate deploy`, isolated smoke test with the tunnel deliberately **off**) and a **short after-close cutover** (fresh final backup → stop old app → `pg_restore --clean` the dump + `Expand-Archive` the matching `uploads_*.zip` → move the tunnel by copying `.cloudflared/` or creating a fresh one → repoint tablets only if the LAN IP changed → NSSM services + re-register the backup/maintenance/restore-drill tasks). Includes a **headless mini-PC remote-access** section (§1a) — the box runs server + tunnel only with no monitor, so it documents: borrow a screen for the one-time install, buy a ~$5 HDMI dummy plug, rely on the NSSM services to keep it alive with nobody logged in, and **never** port-forward RDP. Remote access is a committed **Tailscale + Windows RDP** path (private encrypted mesh, reachable from anywhere, free tier, nothing exposed publicly) with step-by-step setup: enable RDP (requires **Windows 11 Pro** to host — Home can't), create a strong RDP login, install Tailscale on both the mini PC and laptop under one account, **disable key expiry** on the headless box so it never drops off, connect via `mstsc` to the `100.x.x.x` address, and verify from off-LAN on a mobile hotspot; plus an optional subnet-routing note for reaching the shop LAN's printers remotely. Also includes a verification set (LAN + off-Wi-Fi public checks, test print, $1 card charge, end-to-end online order, cold-boot power-loss test), an old-PC **decommission** section (keep its tunnel/services disabled so it can't contend, final labelled backup, wipe secrets/DB), and a migration-specific gotcha table (zone-secret mismatch exposing staff pages, dual tunnels fighting, missing `uploads` zip, missing `.env`, UTC timezone, PostgreSQL version mismatch, `JWT_SECRET` change logging everyone out). Cross-linked from [database-backup.md](docs/database-backup.md)'s "rebuild on a new PC" note. Also includes a **"Using an AI agent to help set up the mini PC"** section for driving the migration with an AI coding agent on the box: point it at [AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md) + these docs and have it follow them step by step; a green-light list (prerequisites, `git clone`/`npm install`/`build`, empty DB + `prisma migrate deploy`, smoke test, `pg_restore` + uploads unzip, NSSM services + scheduled tasks, verification) and a hands-off list the human must own (copy the un-backed-up `.env` itself and never let the agent fabricate one or paste secrets into chat; confirm the old PC's `cloudflared` is stopped before the agent starts the new tunnel, since one machine can't see the other; and don't let it touch money/zone/payment code, which is out of scope for a move). Docs-only — no app code touched.

### Changed
- **Centralized the customer-facing shop name so a future rebrand is one env var, not ~15 file edits.** "DreamyCafe" is currently a project name; when the cafe adopts its real name/domain the customer-facing strings need to change without touching the project/dev identity (scripts, agent rules, package names). Added [`src/lib/brand.js`](src/lib/brand.js) exporting `BRAND_NAME` (reads `NEXT_PUBLIC_BUSINESS_NAME`, falls back to `'DreamyCafe'`) and `BRAND_TAGLINE` (`NEXT_PUBLIC_BUSINESS_TAGLINE`), and wired every hardcoded customer-facing occurrence to it: the storefront homepage (header/hero/about/footer/copyright, [StorefrontHome.js](src/components/public/StorefrontHome.js)), the online order header ([PublicOnlineOrderPage.js](src/components/public/PublicOnlineOrderPage.js)), the order-tracking header + thank-you ([PublicTrackOrderPage.js](src/components/public/PublicTrackOrderPage.js)), the privacy policy title/meta/header/body ([privacy/page.js](src/app/privacy/page.js)), and the root metadata — tab title, description, iOS web-app title ([layout.js](src/app/layout.js)). **Receipts/Z-reports were left on their existing separate `DOCKET_BRAND_NAME` var** (a shop may print a different legal/trading name than the web brand) — no behavior change there. **Static files that can't read env vars stay manual** and are documented, not changed: [public/manifest.json](public/manifest.json), [public/offline.html](public/offline.html), and the separate `marketing/` Cloudflare Pages site. Added [docs/rebrand-checklist.md](docs/rebrand-checklist.md) laying out the fast path (env vars), the manual static edits, the domain/infra + Square/Stripe dashboard changes, and — importantly — the **leave-as-is list** (repo name, package.json, AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md, scripts, `window.DREAMYCAFE_ORDER_ORIGIN` var name) that must NOT be renamed. Verified: `next build` clean, full route table compiled, and a grep confirming zero hardcoded "DreamyCafe" remains in the public components with every consumer importing `BRAND_NAME`. No money math, payment, auth, or zone code touched.

### Fixed
- **Lower-stakes sweep — reservations, combos, and the printing/print-queue failure paths; one latency bug on the hot checkout + webhook paths fixed, one validation gap closed, and the rest confirmed sound.** The remaining un-audited subsystems (all LAN-only or non-fatal-by-design, no direct money movement). Traced each end-to-end; most came out clean, with two real fixes. **(1) Printing — blocking `await` froze the till for up to 10s on a printer-offline (real):** `POST /api/orders` and `fulfillPaidOrder` (both webhooks' ack path) each did `await printOrderDockets(order)` as their last side effect — but `printOrderDockets` **never throws** (each station is independently try/caught and enqueues a `PrintJob` for reprint on failure), so the `await` bought nothing and *cost* the full `PRINT_TIMEOUT_MS` (5s) **per offline station** blocking the HTTP response — a ~10s checkout hang on the POS tablet when a printer is unplugged, and on the online path a slow webhook ack that invites the payment provider to retry-deliver (harmless — the status-flip is idempotent — but pointless). Changed both to fire-and-forget with `.catch()`, matching the receipt/drawer/loyalty/stock side effects already sitting right beside them in the same functions; dockets still print on success and still queue on failure, the order/fulfillment is recorded either way, and the response no longer waits on hardware. **The rest of the print path was confirmed robust and left alone:** `printDocket` has a hard socket timeout + double-settle guard; `printOrderDockets` enqueues per-station failures; the reprint route (`POST /api/print-queue/[id]/reprint`) clears the job on success and re-enqueues on repeat failure; `enqueueFailedDocket` upserts on the `(orderId, station)` unique key so re-failures update rather than duplicate; every queue DB op is try/caught. **(2) Reservations — server-side party-size cap was missing (real, low-severity):** the reservation form caps party size at `max="100"`, but neither `POST /api/reservations` nor `validateSeriesPayload` enforced an upper bound server-side — a direct API call could send an unbounded `partySize`, and `parseInt` of a value past 2³¹ would overflow the `Int` column into an unhelpful 500. Added `if (size > 100)` to both write paths (single + series), mirroring the form's cap. **Deliberately did *not* add a past-date guard** — staff back-entering a reservation that already occurred is a legitimate record-keeping workflow, so rejecting past dates would break real use. The recurrence engine (`lib/recurrence.js`) was confirmed **DST-safe by construction** (builds occurrences via local-time `new Date(y, mo, d, h, m)`, never UTC arithmetic on wall-clock times; `weeksBetween` uses `Math.round` to absorb 23/25-hour DST weeks) and the series top-up's `skipDuplicates` is backed by a real `@@unique([seriesId, scheduledAt])` constraint — so overlapping regeneration can't double-book. **(3) Combos — no bug; both boundaries already correct.** The upcharge math (`ComboBuilderModal`) is clean: `sizeDeltaFor` floors at `max(D(0), chosen − cheapest)` so a smaller size can't discount and picking the cheapest adds nothing, add-ons sum modifier prices, all through Decimal + `roundCents`. The flagged "does the server recompute combo prices?" concern resolves correctly by **design boundary**: the POS `POST /api/orders` trusts client line prices, but it is **LAN-only and behind the staff login** — `src/proxy.js` allows only `/api/orders/online` + webhooks + voucher-validate for public-zone POST, so the bare POS route 404s from the internet; recomputing there would only defend against a trusted insider who could just as easily apply a discount. The **online** path *does* recompute every price server-side and **hard-rejects combos** (`onlineOrderItems.js`: `if (product.isCombo) return { ok:false, error:'Product not found' }`, covered by the existing test suite). Verified: `npm test` **51/51 green** · `npm run build` clean · a probe confirming the party-size cap accepts 1–100 and rejects 101/999999 across both reservation write paths. No money math, discount/loyalty/voucher math, zone check, or price-recompute guard touched — the two fixes are a side-effect-scheduling change and an input bound.

### Removed
- **Accounting rollup audit → tip feature removed entirely (owner's call), which dissolves the one real finding at the source.** Fourth and final stop on the "which side didn't we audit" review: the accounting / P&L rollup (`src/lib/accounting.js`). **The reconciliation itself is sound** — a runnable probe through the *real* `aggregateAccounting` / `buildPnl` math proved every structural identity closes: `net == gross − refunds`, the daily breakdown sums exactly to the headline gross/refunds/net, and both `byPaymentMethod` and `bySource` tie back to gross — all on Decimal with `roundCents` at every boundary, no float drift; refunds correctly retain the original `total` and flip status to `REFUNDED`. **The one genuine finding was economic, not arithmetic:** `order.total` *included* `tipAmount`, so tips flowed gross → net → **`netProfit`**, and since the codebase has **no tip-payout mechanism anywhere** (no tip pool, no distribution, no tip-as-expense entry — grepped exhaustively), collected tips were being booked as owner profit with nothing paying them back out (probe showed netProfit $93 vs true-ex-tips $85 on an $8-tip sample — overstatement == `tipsTotal`, scaling with tip volume). Rather than net tips out of the P&L, the owner chose to **remove the tip feature outright** (not needed now or planned). So the collection path is fully severed: the POS `POST /api/orders` no longer reads `body.tipAmount` and no longer adds it to `total` (persists `tipAmount = 0` for the retained column); `pos/page.js` no longer forwards it; and `CheckoutModal` loses its tip state, `TIP_PRESETS` (dropped from `checkout/constants.js`, `QUICK_AMOUNTS` kept), the preset handler, the tip UI block, the grand-total term, and the `CheckoutTotalsPanel` tip row/prop. **The `tipAmount` DB column and all read-side displays are deliberately kept** (`accounting.js` aggregation + CSV, `printer.js`, `zReport.js`, `OrderDetailModal.js`, `AccountingSummaryCard.js`) — every one is a historical read guarded by `?? 0` / `gt(…, 0)`, so past orders that genuinely collected a tip still reconcile and print correctly, while everything new reads $0; removing the column would rewrite history. The online path never had tips (no field), so it's untouched. Updated the two money-exactness checks (`test/money.test.js`, `scripts/test-money.js`) that used `+ tip` as a representative addend to match the current `subtotal + surcharge − discount` formula (10.10 + 0.15 − 2.00 = 8.25). Verified: `npm test` **51/51 green**, `npm run build` clean (full route table, zero warnings), and a post-fix reconciliation probe confirmed a fresh trading day now carries `tipsTotal = 0` with **no tip baked into `netProfit`**. This clears the last money-/integrity-critical subsystem — fulfillment, purchasing/receivings, payroll, and now the accounting rollup are all audited. No other money math, discount/loyalty/voucher math, zone check, or price-recompute guard touched.

### Fixed
- **Payroll hours — dedicated audit of the clock/hours path; overnight shifts no longer silently read as 0h, and negative-duration shifts can't poison the total.** Third stop on the "which side didn't we audit" review. **First, the severity correction:** the audit confirmed there is **no wage multiplication anywhere** — the payroll report outputs *hours only* (`formatHours` → `Xh Ym`; wages are entered separately as a lump-sum **Expense**), and the only `hourly*` money code (`hourlyMap` in `/api/reports`) is unrelated sales-by-hour already on Decimal. So this was **never a money-rule violation** — but the hours themselves feed a report a human pays staff from, and a runnable probe proved two real bugs (plus a benign one): (1) **Overnight shift recorded 0h (highest impact):** `payrollDaysFromRecords` bucketed each clock record into a per-day array **before** pairing, so an IN at 22:00 and its OUT at 02:00 next day landed in different day-buckets, never paired, and **both days showed 0h** — a late-shift worker who clocked out after midnight silently got paid nothing (reachable via the normal live clock-in/out flow). Rewrote it to **pair across the whole date-sorted record list first**, attribute each completed shift to the day it **started**, then bucket — so a real 22:00→02:00 shift now records its true 4h on the start day. (A shift whose OUT falls outside the queried range still dangles — an inherent limit of the range query, documented in-code.) (2) **Negative-duration shift subtracted from the total:** the `clock-inout` start/end form stamps both times on the *same* day via `setHours`, so entering start 22:00 / end 02:00 stored an OUT *before* the IN → a **−20h** shift that reduced the payroll total. Fixed at both ends: `payrollDaysFromRecords` now clamps each shift with `Math.max(0, duration)` (and `TimesheetsEditor`'s total does the same, so a bad pair can't subtract there either), **and** the `clock-inout` route now rejects `end <= start` (400) so the bad record can't be written in the first place — an overnight shift spanning midnight is recorded via live clock-in/out or the manager editor across two days. (3) **Forgotten clock-out** (dangling IN) still contributes 0h but is listed as an open shift — left as-is (inventing an end time would be worse), just confirmed it can't go negative. `pairClockEventsForDisplay` deliberately keeps returning the **raw signed** duration (the honest primitive); clamping is each caller's explicit decision. Locked in with **7 new `node:test` cases** (`test/clockPairs.test.js`: normal, overnight→start-day, negative→clamp-0, dangling, two-shifts, exact 7×7.5h=52.5h no-drift, raw-duration) — **51/51 green**; `npm run build` clean; a rolled-back live probe confirmed overnight 0h→4h and same-day-bad −20h→0h. No money math, discount/loyalty/voucher math, zone check, or price-recompute guard touched.

### Fixed
- **Purchasing / stock receivings — dedicated audit of the inventory-mutation path; two atomicity fixes + a referential-integrity FK.** Second stop on the "which side didn't we audit" review. The receivings feature (default-OFF, but writes real inventory + P&L purchase totals the moment it's on) bumps `StockItem.quantity` on save and reverses it on delete, and had never had the concurrency pass the cash/POS paths got. **The trace cleared the headline concern:** my flagged "double-apply on delete" is actually defended on both ends — the client removes the row optimistically (no double-click) and the server's `prisma.stockReceiving.delete` throws `P2025` on a racing second delete, rolling back its reversal — so no live double-reversal. Two real issues remained, both the same "read-modify-write instead of atomic DB write" class the app fixes everywhere else: (1) **Lost update on a never-counted item (`POST /api/stock-receivings`):** when a `StockItem.quantity` was currently `null`, the bump did `findUnique` then **set** `{ quantity: n }` (a prior-read set, not an atomic op) — so two receivings for the same brand-new item saved concurrently both read `null` and both set, and the second **overwrote** the first, losing its units (same shape as the cash "two tills opened at once" bug). Replaced the read-then-set with a single atomic `UPDATE ... SET "quantity" = COALESCE("quantity",0) + n` (null bootstraps to `n`, existing increments — in one write, no lost update; a non-existent id no-ops). (2) **Non-atomic delete reversal (`DELETE /api/stock-receivings/[id]`):** the reversal was `Math.max(0, item.quantity - line.quantity)` then `update` (read-modify-write) — currently safe only by accident (the `P2025` guard above), fragile if that ever changes. Replaced with the floored atomic `UPDATE ... SET "quantity" = GREATEST("quantity" - n, 0) WHERE ... AND "quantity" IS NOT NULL` (matches `POST /api/orders`; the `IS NOT NULL` guard preserves the old "leave a never-counted item untouched" behaviour instead of materialising a 0). (3) **Missing FK (`StockReceivingLine.stockItemId`):** it was a bare `Int?` with no relation, so a `StockItem` could be deleted while receivings referenced its id, and the reversal would silently skip (or, if ids were ever reused, reverse the wrong item). Added a real `stockItem StockItem? @relation(onDelete: SetNull)` (+ back-reference) so deleting a stock item severs the link cleanly and the reversal's existing `if (!line.stockItemId) continue` correctly skips it. Migration `20260708041127_add_stockitem_fk_on_receiving_line` adds the FK and **pre-nulls any orphaned references first** so `migrate deploy` can't fail on live data. Verified: `prisma migrate dev` + `generate` clean, `npm run build` clean, `npm test` 44/44, and a live rolled-back functional check (`scratchpad`) proving all six cases — null→n bump, re-increment, existing +n, reverse-floors-at-0, reverse-skips-null, reverse normal −n. No money math, discount/loyalty/voucher math, zone check, or price-recompute guard touched.

### Fixed
- **Online fulfillment path (`src/lib/fulfillment.js`) — dedicated audit of the live Square money path; two concurrency/robustness fixes, and confirmation the rest is sound.** Followed the "which side didn't we audit" review into `fulfillPaidOrder`, the function every paid online order (Square webhook, Stripe webhook, success-page poll fallback, and the $0-voucher direct path) runs through. **The end-to-end trace mostly *cleared* it:** the status-flip is an atomic conditional `updateMany` (only one racing caller proceeds; the rest no-op), so every side effect runs exactly once; the live stock/voucher/free-item holds are all claimed **atomically at order creation** inside `claimOnlineReservations`'s `$transaction` (stock guarded `stock >= qty`, voucher usage-limit-guarded, stamp-redeem guarded `stampsRedeemed < FLOOR(stampsCollected/9)`), so the reservation-skip block in fulfillment is dead for all current callers; and `printOrderDockets` is internally try/caught (enqueues a failed docket, never throws), so a printer-offline can't 500 the webhook. Two real issues remained and are fixed: (1) **Customer-attach race (real):** two orders from the *same new phone* fulfilling concurrently each win their own status-flip, then race `Customer.findUnique`→`create`; `Customer.phone` is `@unique`, so the loser threw `P2002`, aborting fulfillment **before** its loyalty-stamp accrual — the order recovered on the provider's webhook retry but that order's stamps were silently lost. Now the create catches `P2002` and re-fetches the winning customer row (the same idempotency pattern `POST /api/orders` uses for its unique-index race), and customer-attach degrades gracefully (proceeds without a customer rather than aborting) in the impossible remaining case. (2) **Latent un-floored stock decrement (defensive):** the (currently unreachable) reservation-skip block decremented stock with a plain `decrement` guarded only by `stock > 0`, so stock 1 × qty 3 would have gone to −2 if that path were ever reached — the exact oversell bug the POS-order audit already fixed. Realigned it to the floored atomic `GREATEST("stock" - qty, 0)` raw update matching `POST /api/orders`, removing the trap without removing the fallback. No money math, discount/loyalty/voucher *math*, zone check, or price-recompute guard changed. `npm run build` clean; `npm test` 44/44 green.

### Added
- **A `node:test` unit suite (`npm test`) covering the money, pricing, loyalty, voucher, and zone-security logic — turning the "verify by hand" discipline into machine-enforced regression protection.** Uses Node's **built-in** test runner (no new dependency, per repo rules); test files live in `test/*.test.js`. The `@/` path alias (a webpack/jsconfig alias that doesn't resolve under plain `node`) is made to work at test time by a tiny module-resolution hook (`test/alias-loader.mjs`, registered via `test/register.mjs` and `--import`), and a scoped `test/package.json` (`"type":"module"`) keeps the root package.json untouched. **44 tests, all green; `npm run build` clean.** Coverage: `money` (exact-cent arithmetic, `toCents`/`fromCents` round-trips, proportional split reconciliation, JSON string round-trip — supersedes and extends `scripts/test-money.js`), `orderTotals` (line/subtotal with modifiers + adjustments + the include/exclude-adjustments branch), `loyalty` (the `/9` stamp math, cheapest-eligible free-item selection, eligibility filtering), `voucher` (`parseVoucherInput` validation + `evaluateVoucher` — PERCENT/FIXED/FREE_ITEM, the "discount capped at subtotal so a total can never go negative" invariant, expiry/usage/customer-scope/min-subtotal rejections), `zone` (**the public-zone forgery defense** — a missing/mismatched/case-different `x-dreamy-zone` header is never treated as public), and — the highest-value one — `onlineOrderItems` (**the server-side price-recompute boundary**: proves a tampered client `unitPrice`/size/modifier price is discarded in favour of the DB value, combos/unavailable/out-of-stock/bad-size/bad-modifier are rejected, and inherited category modifiers are suppressed once a product has its own). **Small supporting refactor:** `resolveOnlineOrderItems(rawItems)` now takes an optional second arg `resolveOnlineOrderItems(rawItems, { loadProducts })` defaulting to the existing Prisma query, so the recompute guard is unit-testable without a database — **behaviour is identical when called the existing way** (both call sites, `POST /api/orders/online` and `POST /api/vouchers/validate`, pass no second arg and are unchanged; verified by a clean production build). The live-server `scripts/test-*.js` smoke tests are unchanged and remain manual pre-deploy checks. Documented in AGENTS.md (new "Tests" section). No money/auth/zone *logic* changed — only added tests and an injectable seam.

### Changed
- **Pinned the Stripe and Square API versions, and added an "annual / calendar upkeep" section to the RUNBOOK — longevity hardening for an app that may be frozen and left running.** Two parts, both aimed at the failure modes that bite an *un-maintained* POS: things that decay on a calendar rather than in the code. (1) **API version pinning:** both payment clients were constructed with **no API version**, inheriting whatever the installed SDK defaulted to — the classic silent-breakage vector, since Stripe and Square each *retire* old API versions on a rolling schedule and a bare client would one day start failing with no code change. `getStripe()` (`src/lib/terminal.js`) and the standalone client in `src/app/api/webhooks/stripe/route.js` now pass `{ apiVersion: '2026-05-27.dahlia' }`; `getSquare()` (`src/lib/square.js`) now passes `version: '2026-05-20'` (overrides the `Square-Version` header). **Both strings are the installed SDKs' own current defaults** (`stripe@22.2.0` → `2026-05-27.dahlia`, `square@44.1.0` → `2026-05-20`), so behaviour is unchanged today — the pin just makes a future SDK reinstall or a provider version-sunset a **scheduled** event we control instead of a surprise outage. Verified by instantiating both clients with the pins (Stripe echoes the pinned version back; Square client still exposes `terminal`/`payments`). No money/capture/refund/auth/zone logic touched — only the client version header. Consulted `skills/stripe-best-practices` (`references/security.md`) and `skills/square-best-practices` (the `version` option is documented as the `Square-Version` override). (2) **RUNBOOK §7A "Annual / calendar upkeep":** §7's automated upkeep is all self-alerting via healthchecks.io; this new section is the opposite — domain renewal, card-on-file expiry (registrar/Cloudflare/providers), payment-credential/Square-token expiry, keeping the off-machine `.env` **and** `~/.cloudflared/` copies current, the payment-API-version sunset check (with the two pinned strings recorded), and a yearly Node/Windows EOL glance. These expire on a calendar, throw **no error until the day they fail**, and no dead-man's switch watches them — so they now live on an explicit list for anyone planning to walk away from the app.

### Changed
- **Prefill the customer's phone on Square's hosted checkout so they don't re-enter it.** Square's hosted payment-link page has a mandatory **Contact** block (phone/email/name) and **no API toggle to hide or make it optional** — verified against the installed `square` SDK: `CheckoutOptions` exposes only `askForShippingAddress` (address, not contact), so the phone requirement cannot be removed in code, only prefilled. Since our own `/order` form already collects the customer's phone, we now pass it into `squareCreateOnlineCheckout` and set `prePopulatedData.buyerPhoneNumber` on `checkout.paymentLinks.create`, so the required field arrives filled instead of blank (customer confirms rather than retypes). Threaded `phone` through the route → `createOnlineCheckout` params; added a small local `auPhoneToE164()` that maps the already-normalized `0XXXXXXXXX` AU number to `+61XXXXXXXXX` (E.164, what Square's `+61` selector expects), returning `null` for anything unexpected so prepopulation is simply omitted rather than sending a malformed value. `PrePopulatedData` / `buyerPhoneNumber` field confirmed present in the SDK; conversion unit-checked (`0412345678`→`+61412345678`, landline `02…` and malformed inputs handled); both files parse clean as ESM. Does **not** change the fact that phone is required (that's Square's config) — only saves the re-entry.
