Only prefills what the customer already gave us; no money/auth/zone logic touched. **Not yet verified live** (production-only Square env — see auto-complete note below); needs a Square **sandbox** checkout to confirm the field renders prefilled end-to-end.

### Changed
- **App online orders now auto-complete on Square so they leave the dashboard's "Active" tab — matching reader orders.** Square shows in-person **Terminal** orders (`terminal.checkouts.create`) as **Completed / In store**, but online orders go through **Payment Links** (`checkout.paymentLinks.create`), which leave the Square order **OPEN with no fulfillment** — so every paid app order sat forever in **Payments & invoices → Orders → Active** as "New". Added `completeSquareOnlineFulfillment(squareOrderId)` in `src/lib/square.js`: it `orders.get`s the order and, unless a `COMPLETED` fulfillment already exists (idempotent no-op), does a sparse `orders.update` attaching a `PICKUP` fulfillment in state `COMPLETED` (using the order `version` for optimistic concurrency + an `idempotencyKey`). Called best-effort from `src/app/api/webhooks/square/route.js` after `fulfillPaidOrder`, using the `payment.orderId` the webhook already carries — it's **cosmetic on Square's side only and never throws**, so a failure can't break the money-critical DB fulfillment or the webhook ack. No change to money, capture/refund, DB order status, auth, or zone logic. Request/`Fulfillment` field shapes verified against the installed `square` SDK (`orders.get`/`orders.update` present; `type`/`state`/`pickupDetails` valid; `PICKUP`/`COMPLETED` are documented enum values); both files parse clean as ESM. **Not yet verified live:** the only configured environment is `SQUARE_ENV=production` with live credentials, so the actual `orders.update` round-trip was deliberately **not** exercised against real Square data — needs one paid Square **sandbox** order (dev server + browser test card firing `payment.updated`) to confirm the order drops out of Active end-to-end before this is considered proven. Consulted `skills/square-best-practices` (`references/online.md` documents that Payment Link orders stay OPEN).

### Added
- **Australian compliance groundwork: valid-tax-invoice guard, a `/privacy` policy page, and a go-live legal checklist.** A pre-production pass over AU rules a café POS touches (not legal advice — flagged for professional sign-off). Two code fixes + docs. (1) **Tax invoice validity (ATO):** the printed receipt was always titled "TAX INVOICE / RECEIPT" but the ABN line only printed when `RECEIPT_ABN` was set — so going live without an ABN would print something calling itself a tax invoice without the ABN that legally makes it one. `src/lib/printer.js` now prints "TAX INVOICE / RECEIPT" **only when an ABN is present**, and plain "RECEIPT" otherwise (blank/whitespace ABN counts as absent via the existing `?.trim() || null`). (2) **Privacy (APPs):** the public site collects customer **name + phone** for loyalty/online orders and had **no privacy policy**. Added a static `src/app/privacy/page.js` (Australian Privacy Principles-oriented: what's collected, how it's used, who it's shared with, retention, your rights + OAIC pointer) with clear `[REVIEW]` placeholders where the owner must supply specifics and get wording checked. Wired `/privacy` into the public-zone allowlist in `src/proxy.js` (else the internet zone would redirect it to `/`) and linked it from the loyalty page footer. Verified against the running production build: `/privacy` renders 200 on the LAN **and** through the public-zone header, while `/pos` still 307-redirects to `/` from the public zone (staff routes stay hidden); page contains the policy heading + APP reference + REVIEW markers; ABN-guard title logic checked for set/empty/null. (3) **Docs:** new "Legal & compliance (Australia)" block in `docs/go-live-checklist.md` — mandatory ABN, GST config, **card-surcharge caution** (must not exceed cost of acceptance; confirm current 2026 RBA/ACCC surcharge-reform position before enabling — the app doesn't cap the rate), fill-in-the-`[REVIEW]` privacy step, and 5-year record-keeping (covered by backups). `npm run build` clean; no money/auth/zone *logic* changed (proxy change is an allowlist addition for a public static page).

### Added
- **Card wait time is now configurable in Admin → Payments (one setting drives both the reader deadline and the POS timeout).** Follow-up to matching the timers: the shared wait window was hardcoded at 120s — now it's a `PaymentSettings.terminalWaitSeconds` field (migration `20260707053032_add_terminal_wait_seconds`, `INT NOT NULL DEFAULT 120`), editable from the **Counter reader** card as a "Card wait time" number input (30–300s, its own Save). The single value flows to both places that must agree: `squareProvider.charge` reads it via `getPaymentSettings()` and sends `deadlineDuration: PT<n>S` to the Square checkout, and the POS threads it into `runTerminalCharge`'s `timeoutSec` (client poll safety-net still trips `POLL_GRACE_SEC` after). Clamped everywhere through `clampTerminalWait` in `src/lib/terminalDeadline.js` (`TERMINAL_WAIT_MIN=30`, `TERMINAL_WAIT_MAX=300`, NaN → 120 default), enforced on write in the `payment-settings` PATCH and again on read, so a bad value can't reach Square. `TERMINAL_DEADLINE_SECONDS` stays the code default/fallback. Verified end-to-end against the running DB: a stored 90 resolves to a `PT90S` deadline and a 90s poll timeout; out-of-range 5000/1 clamp to 300/30; `next build` + `prisma generate` clean. Admin test charge keeps its explicit 60s; no money/capture/refund/auth/zone logic touched. Consulted `skills/square-best-practices` for the `deadlineDuration` shape.

### Changed
- **Matched the reader and app card-wait timers, and finished humanising terminal outcomes.** Two related fixes so a card wait behaves predictably and never shows a raw code. (1) **Matched timers:** the Square reader ran its own ~5-minute checkout deadline while the app's poll gave up at a hardcoded 90s — so on a walk-away the app bailed with its own generic "timed out" long before the reader stopped waiting, and the two could disagree. Added a single source of truth, `src/lib/terminalDeadline.js` (`TERMINAL_DEADLINE_SECONDS = 120` + `terminalDeadlineIso()` → `PT120S`), used **both** for Square's `TerminalCheckout.deadlineDuration` in `squareProvider.charge` **and** as the default `timeoutSec` in `runTerminalCharge`. The client poll now trips only after a small `POLL_GRACE_SEC` (8s) *past* the reader deadline, so in the normal case the **reader's own** cancel fires first and comes back through the `canceled` branch carrying Square's real reason (e.g. `TIMED_OUT`); the local timeout is a pure safety net for a reader that stopped responding. Admin test charge keeps its explicit 60s. (2) **Copy gap:** extended `TERMINAL_ERROR_COPY` in `CheckoutModal.js` to cover Square `cancelReason` values — `TIMED_OUT` → neutral grey "No card tapped", `SELLER_CANCELED` → "Payment canceled" — plus friendlier lines for `Payment not completed` / `Could not finalize payment`; timeouts are now styled as a neutral cancel (grey ✕) rather than an alarming red failure, since "no tap happened" isn't an error. Unknown reasons still fall back to the raw text so nothing is hidden. Presentational + timing only — no change to money, capture/refund, auth, or zone logic; consulted `skills/square-best-practices` for the `deadlineDuration` (ISO-8601) shape. `next build` compiles clean; deadline numbers verified internally consistent (charge `PT120S` = poll 120s, safety net 128s).

### Changed
- **Prettier POS "Payment failed" panel — distinguishes a cancel from a real failure and humanises Square's raw status codes.** When a terminal charge is cancelled on the reader, `runTerminalCharge` surfaces the provider's raw string (e.g. `BUYER_CANCELED`) straight into the checkout overlay, which read as an alarming red "Payment failed" for what is actually a routine buyer cancel. Added a small `TERMINAL_ERROR_COPY` map + `terminalErrorCopy()` helper in `src/components/pos/CheckoutModal.js` that maps known outcomes (`BUYER_CANCELED`/`CANCELED` → neutral grey "Payment canceled", plus friendlier copy for timeout, decline, and reader-offline) to a title + one-line detail, falling back to the raw text for anything unknown so no information is lost. Redesigned the failed-state panel accordingly: a soft circular icon (grey ✕ for a cancel, red ⚠️ for a genuine failure), a colour-matched title, the humanised detail line, and the retry button relabelled "Try again". Presentational only — no change to the charge/finalize/cancel flow, money, auth, or zone logic. `next build` compiles clean. **Note:** the receipt-method prompt shown *on the Square reader* after a successful tap is a device-side Square Terminal setting (Settings → Checkout on the reader), not controlled by DreamyCafe.

### Added
- **`RUNBOOK.md` — a single operations runbook so the system is runnable by someone who isn't the author.** The main long-term risk for a solo-built POS isn't the code, it's the **bus factor**: eight thorough guides already exist in `docs/`, but they're scattered and organised by *topic*, not by *"the shop is broken, what do I do."* Added a top-level `RUNBOOK.md` that is the single panic-time entry point: §0 orientation (the four moving parts — PostgreSQL / DreamyCafeApp / DreamyCafeTunnel / the kiosk — and the golden rule that **cash sales work on localhost without internet, tunnel, or card reader**); a 60-second health check (`Get-Service` + `localhost:3000/pos` + `/api/health`); the start/stop/restart commands; and **incident playbooks** ordered most-likely-cause-first for the real failure modes (nothing loads → check services in dependency order; card won't process → it's the reader/provider, not us; online down but till fine → restart the tunnel; printer offline → order still saved; PC rebooted → wait 2 min for auto-start; DB corrupt → safety-dump then `find-good-backup.ps1` then human restore; "backup failed" email → read the off-site alert, disk-low is urgent). It deliberately **indexes** the `docs/` guides rather than duplicating them (every playbook ends by pointing into the authoritative doc), and §6 calls out the one irreplaceable thing — **`.env` is in no backup and no git**, so a rebuild needs code + `.dump` + `uploads_*.zip` + a separately-kept `.env`. Verified every referenced file exists and every deep-link anchor resolves to a real heading. No code change; documentation only.

### Added
- **Monthly DB/disk maintenance + a disk-space alarm on the backup — the last 24/7-longevity gaps.** After the index + payload-cap work, a machine-level audit found three slow creeps that would eventually degrade a mini-PC running for years, and one hard-stop failure mode. New `scripts/db-maintenance.ps1` (registered monthly via `scripts/register-maintenance-task.ps1`, Sundays 3:00 AM — clear of the 9:30 PM backup and trading hours) does three cheap jobs: (1) **prunes `PrintJob` rows older than 90 days** — that table gains a row per failed/attempted print and nothing else ever deleted them; (2) **`VACUUM ANALYZE`** — reclaims dead tuples and refreshes planner stats so indexed queries stay fast, the safety net for autovacuum and the classic reason a Postgres POS "gets slow after a year"; (3) **trims `logs/desktop.log`** — the one log NSSM doesn't rotate (app-*/tunnel-* rotate at 10 MB; desktop.log was already ~1 MB and unbounded), capped at 5 MB keeping the last 2000 lines. It reuses the same `BACKUP_HEALTHCHECK_URL` dead-man's-switch (pings on success, `/fail` with the real psql error on any failure). Separately, `scripts/backup-db.ps1` now carries a **disk-space guard**: it checks free space on the backup drive each run and soft-fails the healthcheck below `$MinFreeGB` (5 GB) — a full disk is the one failure that hard-stops the shop (PostgreSQL refuses writes → no sales *and* no backups), so this alerts off-site while there's still room to act. Verified live: maintenance ran green end-to-end (PrintJob prune 0 rows — confirming the case-sensitive `"PrintJob"` identifier now survives to psql via a temp `.sql` file rather than being lowercased by shell quoting; VACUUM ANALYZE completed; log-trim check ran) and the disk guard read 75.1 GB free with `lowDisk=False`. Both scripts are ASCII-only + CRLF and parse clean. Remaining longevity items are hardware/OS config, not code (a UPS against power-loss DB corruption, Windows Update active-hours so it doesn't reboot mid-trade, time sync) and belong on the go-live checklist; documented alongside the maintenance schedule in `docs/database-backup.md`.

### Fixed
- **Two unbounded list queries that would slow the app over years — capped, with server-side customer search.** Follow-up to the index work: audited every list endpoint for payloads that grow without limit (the thing that actually makes a long-running POS feel sluggish — a multi-MB response the tablet must download/parse/render). Found two. (1) **`GET /api/orders`** did `findMany` with no `take`, so without a `date` filter it would return the **entire order history** + all items/modifiers in one response. Every current UI caller passes a date (one day), so it's fine today, but the endpoint was a latent footgun — added a `take: 500` safety cap so a broad/unfiltered query can never dump the whole table. (2) **`GET /api/customers`** (no-phone branch, used by the manager Loyalty Customers page) loaded **every customer ever** — unbounded with loyalty signups. Capped at the newest 1000 and added optional **server-side search** (`?q=` over name/phone, case-insensitive) plus a true `count()` total, so any customer stays findable regardless of the cap; the response shape became `{ customers, total }` and the manager page was rewired to search server-side (250ms debounce, abortable) and show the real total. POS/public customer lookups were already single-row by phone and are unaffected; `GET /api/sales` was already properly paginated (`skip`/`take`/`count`). Verified live against the running app: customers returns `{customers,total}` with `total=43` independent of the cap, `?q=Jaf` server-searches to 2 matches, and `GET /api/orders?date=…`/`?count=1` still return the same array/count shape; `npm run build` clean. Reads-only + payload-size changes, no money/auth/zone logic touched.

### Changed
- **Added database indexes on the tables that grow forever, so reports stay fast after years of trading.** A 24/7-longevity audit found the app has **no memory leak** — the in-memory rate limiter self-prunes, printer sockets open/close per print, and all React intervals/state are client-side (reset on reload), so server RAM stays flat over months/years. The real long-horizon risk is *query speed*: `Order`, `OrderItem`, `OrderItemModifier`, `ClockRecord`, and `CashMovement` only ever grow, yet the schema had **zero `@@index`** — every report/history query filtered `Order.createdAt` and joined `OrderItem.orderId` with no index (PostgreSQL doesn't auto-index foreign keys). Invisible at today's 87 orders, but a sequential scan at 100k+ (a few years in) would make reports visibly lag. Added `@@index` on the hot columns (migration `20260706150717_add_indexes_for_growing_tables`, 12 indexes): `Order.createdAt`/`status`/`source`/`customerId`/`userId`, `OrderItem.orderId`/`productId`, `OrderItemModifier.orderItemId`, `ClockRecord.(userId,timestamp)`+`timestamp`, `CashMovement.cashSessionId`+`createdAt`. Verified the planner now uses `Order_createdAt_idx` for a date-range scan (Bitmap Index Scan). Indexes only speed reads and are applied automatically on deploy via `prisma migrate deploy`; no app-logic change. Disk growth remains negligible (~0.5-1 GB per 5 years; DB is 10 MB today), and existing retention already bounds backups and service logs.

### Added
- **Backup now also captures product images (they live on disk, not in the DB).** Audit of "what's actually in the backup" surfaced a gap: product photos are written to `public/uploads/products/` on disk and the DB only stores the *path* (`Product.imageUrl`), so a DB-only restore on a fresh machine would leave broken images. `scripts/backup-db.ps1` now archives that folder to `backups/uploads_<stamp>.zip` (same timestamp as the dump so images and their DB rows restore together), mirrors it to Google Drive with the same size-verification as the dump, and prunes it under the same 30/90-day + keep-7 rules. Best-effort: an image-archive failure warns but never fails the DB backup. (Current impact is nil — 8 image files exist on disk but 0 products reference one yet — but this closes the hole before the first real product photo.) Verified: a run produces both the `.dump` and a `uploads_*.zip` containing all 7 PNGs, both mirrored and size-verified. **Also documented what the backup does NOT contain** in `docs/database-backup.md` §1a — most importantly **`.env` (all secrets: DB URL, JWT, Stripe/Square keys, zone secret) is in no backup and no git**, so a full rebuild on a new PC needs the `.dump` + `uploads_*.zip` **and** a separately-kept copy of `.env` + the app code from git. Restore docs now include the `Expand-Archive` step for images and an `.env` reminder.

### Added
- **Backup: diagnosable failures (quarantine + captured error) and a stale-backup-set alarm.** Closes two follow-on gaps: a failed dump was hard-deleted with no evidence of *why*, and nothing warned when new backups kept failing so you'd silently lean on one aging copy. (1) **Quarantine instead of delete + capture the real error:** when a dump fails the `pg_restore -l` integrity check, `scripts/backup-db.ps1` now moves it to `backups\quarantine\<name>.dump.corrupt` (out of every "newest good backup" scan, so it can't be restored by mistake) rather than deleting it, and **captures `pg_restore`'s real stderr** into the `/fail` alert — flattened out of PowerShell's `NativeCommandError` envelope so you get a clean actionable reason (e.g. *"input file does not appear to be a valid tar archive"* = truncated/mid-write, or a version message = tool/server skew) instead of a bare "integrity check failed." (2) **Freshness guard:** every run reports the age of the newest verified-good dump and how many are retained, and pings `/fail` if that newest-good is older than `$MaxGoodAgeHrs` (48h) — so a decaying backup set alerts *before* you're down to one stale copy, not after. Verified: full backup still runs green and logs the freshness line; a planted corrupt dump is quarantined (not deleted), its real `pg_restore` reason is captured clean (no PS envelope), and the good-dump scan correctly ignores the quarantined file and keeps the older good one. Docs updated in `docs/database-backup.md` (quarantine + freshness behaviour, three new troubleshooting rows); script stays ASCII-only + CRLF, parses clean.

### Added
- **Backup: `scripts/find-good-backup.ps1` — newest-restorable-backup finder with automatic fallback.** Answers "if a backup is corrupt, how do I fall back to a working one?" It scans the local `backups\` folder **and** the Google Drive copy newest-first, verifies each dump with `pg_restore -l` (proves the archive can actually be read, not just that a file exists), and reports the newest one that passes plus the exact `pg_restore` restore command — so if last night's dump is corrupt it walks back to the newest good one for you, off-site copies included. Deliberately **read-only**: it never restores anything, because a restore overwrites live data (`--clean`) and a false "corrupt" signal auto-triggering that would be more dangerous than the problem it solves — so restoring stays a human step (copy the printed command). This complements the existing safeguards (bad dumps are deleted at creation, the keep-7 floor guarantees several good restore points, the quarterly drill re-verifies) by covering the remaining case: on-disk rot discovered at restore time. Verified: happy path picks the newest good dump and stops early; the fallback path correctly rejects a planted corrupt "newest" dump (garbage that passes the size gate but fails `pg_restore -l`) and selects the older good one; script is ASCII-only + CRLF and parses clean. Documented as the "First: find the newest restorable backup" step in `docs/database-backup.md` §5.

### Added
- **Backup: off-site (Google Drive) verification + failure alerting** — closing the two operational gaps flagged in the backup audit. (1) **Off-site verification:** the Google Drive mirror was fire-and-forget `Copy-Item` — "copied into the synced folder" was reported as success even if Drive was paused/signed-out so nothing actually uploaded. Now `scripts/backup-db.ps1` verifies the copy landed at the **exact source byte size** (catches a partial/failed copy) and checks that **`GoogleDriveFS` is actually running** (else files just pile up locally and the off-site copy silently goes stale). Google uploads in the background and the local filesystem genuinely can't confirm cloud receipt, so this verifies everything that *is* observable and treats "copied but Drive not running" as a soft failure rather than a green result. (2) **Failure alerting (dead-man's switch):** an optional `BACKUP_HEALTHCHECK_URL` (healthchecks.io ping URL, read from env or `.env`; unset = disabled) is pinged on success and `<url>/fail` on any error — including a corrupt dump, a failed local backup, or an off-site mirror that didn't complete. Because the alert lives off-site, healthchecks.io also emails the owner when a nightly success ping **never arrives** (PC off, scheduled task deleted) — the one failure mode an in-script alert can't catch, and the whole point of a dead-man's switch. Same mental model as the existing UptimeRobot setup, no SMTP creds in the repo, zero new dependencies. Verified on this machine: full backup runs green with the verified-copy path; ping routing hits the base URL on success and `/fail` on error with the run log as the body (tested against a local HTTP listener); the `.env` URL fallback reads the key and correctly ignores a commented-out line; script stays ASCII-only + CRLF and parses clean. Docs: new "Get alerted if a backup fails" section + off-site notes + troubleshooting rows in `docs/database-backup.md`; commented `BACKUP_HEALTHCHECK_URL` placeholder added to `.env`.

### Fixed
- **Backup flow: four bugs found in a dedicated audit, one of which could silently leave zero recoverable backups.** The nightly `pg_dump` + Google-Drive-mirror flow was working, but had gaps that only bite the day you need a restore. (1) **Retention prune had no floor (highest severity):** `scripts/backup-db.ps1` deleted *every* local dump older than 30 days unconditionally — so a stretch where dumps stopped being produced (disk full, password rotated, a Postgres major upgrade making the hardcoded `C:\PostgreSQL\bin` tools lag the server) plus normal aging could prune the folder to **empty**. Added a `$KeepMinimum = 7` floor: the 7 newest dumps are always exempt from the age rule, so there is always a fallback even after a run of failures (same floor applied to the 90-day Google Drive prune). (2) **`pg_dump` exit 0 was trusted blindly:** `pg_dump` can exit 0 yet leave a truncated/empty archive (notably on client/server version skew). Now, right after the dump, the script asserts the file exists, is **≥ 1 KB**, and — crucially — is readable by `pg_restore -l` (a real integrity check that the archive can be parsed back). Any failure deletes the bad file (so it can't masquerade as a good backup or count toward the keep-newest floor) and fails the run non-zero. This automatically hardens the **pre-deploy snapshot** too, since `deploy.ps1` gates on this script's exit code — a deploy's "reversible" safety backup can no longer be a silently-corrupt file. (3) **Restore drill could pass on a broken backup:** `scripts/restore-drill.ps1` ran `pg_restore` **without `--exit-on-error`**, so a dump that restored with per-object errors still "passed"; and the sanity query never asserted it returned a real number. Added `--exit-on-error` and a check that the `Order` count is a plain integer, so a drill can no longer give false confidence. Verified on this machine: a real backup runs, verifies, and mirrors to Drive; the min-size gate rejects a 17-byte file; `pg_restore -l` rejects a garbage archive and accepts the real dump; and the keep-newest floor keeps exactly the 7 newest when all 10 test files are past the cutoff. Scripts kept ASCII-only + CRLF. Still-open (documented, not code-fixable here): the Google Drive "mirror" is a local `Copy-Item` and can't confirm the cloud actually received the file, and there is no active alerting when a nightly backup fails — `LastTaskResult` / `scripts/verify-resilience.ps1` remain the signals.

### Added
- **One-command safe live deploy for the 24/7 mini PC** (`deploy.bat` + `scripts/deploy.ps1`). Automates the previously-manual "npm run build, then Restart-Service DreamyCafeApp" with the safe ordering and guardrails: **pre-deploy `pg_dump`** (reuses `scripts/backup-db.ps1`; aborts if the backup fails) -> optional `-Pull` (git fast-forward) -> **`npm run build`** -> **only if the build succeeds**, `Restart-Service DreamyCafeApp` -> **health-check** `localhost:3000/pos` for 200. The key safety property: `next build` compiles into `.next/` without touching the running server, and a **failed build never reaches the restart**, so a broken build can't take the live POS down (the old build keeps serving until a good one replaces it). On a failed health check it dumps the tail of `logs/app-stderr.log` and prints the rollback command (`pg_restore ... --clean <pre-deploy>.dump`). `deploy.bat` self-elevates to Administrator (NSSM restart needs it). Preflight fails fast with a clear message if the service is missing or **Disabled** (e.g. a dev laptop using the manual `start-pos.bat` launcher) — before wasting a backup/build — instead of the cryptic Windows "Cannot start service" error. `.bat`/`.ps1` saved with CRLF line endings (required for cmd.exe). Only the app service restarts (tunnel + Postgres stay up); the only downtime is the ~10-30s service reboot, so deploy during a quiet moment. Verified: PowerShell parses clean, and the build-failure gate correctly aborts before restart (dry-run). Data changes (menu/prices/surcharge/vouchers/settings/provider switch) remain live with no deploy — only code and `.env` changes need this.

### Fixed
- **POS order path: four failure-handling & concurrency bugs found in a dedicated audit.** (1) **Silent order loss / ghost charge (highest severity):** `handleConfirmOrder` in `src/app/pos/page.js` fired the `POST /api/orders` and **never checked the response** — it unconditionally cleared the cart and showed "Order placed!", so any failure (402 card-verify, 500, or a tablet network blip) wiped the cart and faked success while **no order was recorded**; for card, the reader had already charged, leaving a charge with no order/docket. Now it checks `res.ok`, **keeps the cart on failure**, shows an in-app alert (via `usePromptDialog`, no native dialog) that for card warns the card may have been charged and to refund before re-ringing, and returns a success boolean so the checkout modal stays open to retry. (2) **No idempotency — one charge could record two orders:** a retried/double-submitted POST with the same `paymentIntentId` created a duplicate COMPLETED order (inflated revenue, duplicate docket). Added a route **idempotency short-circuit** (if an order already has that `paymentIntentId`, return it instead of creating another) plus a Postgres **partial unique index** `Order_paymentIntentId_key` (migration `20260706050000`, NULLs excluded so cash orders are unaffected) that rejects a racing duplicate; the create's catch maps `P2002` to returning the winning order. (3) **Stock could go negative / oversell:** the decrement guarded only `stock > 0`, so stock 1 × qty 3 → −2, and two tills selling the last unit both passed. Switched to a floored raw update `GREATEST(stock - qty, 0)` — never negative, never blocks a sale. (4) **Loyalty free-item redemption wasn't atomic:** `stampsRedeemed` was incremented unconditionally, so two tills ringing the same customer's last free coffee both redeemed it. Now a conditional raw update (`WHERE stampsRedeemed < FLOOR(stampsCollected/9)`) so only one applies. Verified end-to-end against the running app: duplicate-PI POST returns the existing order with no new row (count unchanged), the unique index rejects a direct duplicate (P2002), floored stock stops at 0, two concurrent redeems apply exactly one, and a normal cash order still rings 201 with correct totals; money + cash self-checks still pass. The trusted-LAN price model is unchanged — this hardens failure/concurrency handling, not the (intentional) client-computed POS totals.

### Fixed
- **Cash drawer reconciliation: three integrity bugs found in a dedicated audit of the cash/till path.** (1) **Orphaned refund payout (silent drawer drift, highest severity):** the refund route logged its cash `OUT` movement against `cashSessionId: session?.id ?? null` — so refunding a cash order while **no till session was open** wrote the movement with a null session, which **no Z-report ever subtracts**. The cash physically left the drawer but `expectedCash` stayed high, so every future close read permanently over by that amount (indistinguishable from theft). Now `POST /api/orders/[id]/refund` **blocks a cash-moving refund when no session is open** (clear 409, before any Stripe/Square refund so nothing partially completes), and the in-transaction write re-checks and rolls back rather than orphan the movement. (2) **Two tills could open at once:** `POST /api/cash` did `findFirst({closedAt:null})` then `create` with no atomicity and no DB constraint, so two near-simultaneous opens both created a session — corrupting every `findFirst({closedAt:null})` afterward. Added a Postgres **partial unique index** `CashSession_single_open` (migration `20260706040000`, `WHERE closedAt IS NULL`) that rejects a 2nd open session at the DB level; the route maps the `P2002` to the existing friendly 409. (3) **Re-closing a session overwrote the count:** `PATCH /api/cash/[id]` had no already-closed guard and computed expected + closed non-atomically. Now it computes and closes in one `$transaction` with a conditional `updateMany where closedAt: null` — a re-submitted close returns 409 instead of stamping a second count, and a movement can't slip between the calc and the close. Verified end-to-end against the running app (double-open→409, re-close→409, index releases on close, refund route wired) and by a new self-check `node --env-file=.env scripts/test-cash-reconciliation.js` (drawer formula balances for sale/refund/split/payout; DB rejects a 2nd open session; orphaned-null-session movement shape asserted). The correct netting already in place is unchanged: a REFUNDED cash sale stays in the window sum and is cancelled by its OUT movement (never double-deducted), and session windows key off `openedAt`/`closedAt` so a shift spanning midnight still balances.

### Fixed
- **Admin → Payments card surcharge: "disabled but still charging" confusion (unsaved-state gap).** The surcharge card showed “No card surcharge is currently applied” the instant you typed `0` in the amount, but that was only the *form* state — the value doesn't persist until the **Save surcharge & brands** button (which sits below the separate Blocked-brands card) is clicked. So it was easy to set 0, navigate away, and have the DB keep the old surcharge, which then still applied at checkout (surcharge is read live from `PaymentSettings` per request — no build/restart needed, this was never a caching issue). Fixes in `src/app/admin/terminal/page.js`: (1) an **On/Off status pill** in the surcharge card header that reflects the *saved* DB value, not the form; (2) an **“Unsaved changes”** warning inside the card whenever the surcharge/brand form differs from what's saved, with the Save button ringed amber while dirty; (3) `handleSaveSurcharge` now normalizes amount `0` → type `''` before sending so “amount 0 = off” round-trips unambiguously, and refreshes the saved-state snapshot on success. No API/schema change — the server already stored `''` when value is 0.

### Added
- **Vouchers / promo codes — full feature, off by default.** The voucher *engine* already existed (`Voucher` model, `evaluateVoucher`, `POST /api/vouchers/validate`, online-checkout wiring) but had no way to create/manage codes and no customer entry field. Built the missing surface, all gated behind a new `FeatureSettings.vouchersEnabled` flag (migration `20260706030000_add_vouchers_enabled`, default **false**) so it stays dormant until switched on in Admin → Settings — nothing changes for a shop that isn't using it. Pieces: (1) **Management API** — `GET/POST /api/vouchers`, `PATCH/DELETE /api/vouchers/[id]`, ADMIN-gated, with shared input validation `parseVoucherInput` in `src/lib/voucher.js` (unique code normalized uppercase, `PERCENT` **capped at 100**, `FREE_ITEM` value forced to 0, optional usage-limit/expiry/min-subtotal/customer; `timesUsed` never hand-editable). (2) **Admin CRUD page** `/admin/vouchers` (list with usage + status pills, create/edit modal, delete) — the nav link appears only when the flag is on. (3) **Customer entry field** on `/order` + `/onlineorder` (`PublicOnlineOrderPage`): a promo-code input that calls `/api/vouchers/validate`, shows the discount line, and is mutually exclusive with free-coffee redemption (server enforces this too). `vouchersEnabled` is surfaced to the customer via the existing public `/api/payment-settings/public` (no new public endpoint). (4) **In-store POS**: server-side support only — `POST /api/orders` now recomputes the discount with `evaluateVoucher` and atomically claims a `timesUsed` (releasing it on card-verify or create failure) if a `voucherCode` is sent; the POS checkout **UI field was deferred** (shop isn't using vouchers yet, and it would thread discount through the split/loyalty math). All voucher paths (validate + online order + POS order) hard-reject when the flag is off. **Bug fixed along the way:** a voucher covering 100% of an online order drove `total` to $0, which Stripe/Square reject → the customer got a generic 500. Now a $0 online order skips the payment processor and is fulfilled directly (marked PENDING, voucher/stamp/stock committed, straight to the success page). Verified end-to-end against the running app: validate (valid/invalid/flag-off), a $2-off order, a 100%-off order (fulfilled at $0, `timesUsed` incremented exactly once), and flag-off rejection on every path; admin routes 403 without an owner session; `node scripts/test-money.js` still passes.

### Changed
- **Square is now the default payment provider on BOTH channels (in-store + online).** The live `PaymentSettings` row was already `provider = SQUARE` / `onlineProvider = SQUARE` (set 2026-07-01), but the code/schema defaults still said `STRIPE`, and the rules docs described the old Stripe-default model. Aligned everything to the decision: flipped the Prisma `@default` from `STRIPE` → `SQUARE` on `PaymentSettings.provider`, `PaymentSettings.onlineProvider`, and `Order.paymentProvider` (migration `20260706023025_change_default_provider_to_square` — defaults-only, no data mutation; existing orders keep the provider stamped at creation, and the app always passes `paymentProvider` explicitly so the default only affects hypothetical no-value inserts). Updated AGENTS.md (Payment Providers + Live Deployment) to state Square runs on both channels, that the **Square webhook is the live fulfillment path** (`SQUARE_WEBHOOK_SIGNATURE_KEY`, `SQUARE_ENV=production`), and that **brand blocking is therefore inactive** (Square supports none on any channel). The Stripe adapters remain in the code and are still selectable via Admin → Payments. `npm run claude:sync` re-synced the Cursor `.mdc`. No runtime app-logic change — provider selection was already a DB setting resolved via `getInPersonProviderName()` / `getOnlineProviderName()`.
- **Hardened Windows services for an unattended mini-PC (cold-boot + crash recovery)** — targeting a shop mini PC that runs 24/7 and cold-boots after power cuts, the NSSM App service had a latent boot race: `npm run start` runs `prisma migrate deploy` first, but the service had **no dependency on PostgreSQL and no crash-restart policy**, so on a power-loss reboot the app could start before Postgres was ready, fail its migration, and stay down until someone noticed. (The Electron desktop launcher already waits for Postgres via `waitForPostgres`, but the NSSM services path — the recommended 24/7 setup — did not.) Fixed in `scripts/install-windows-services.ps1`: auto-detect the version-specific Postgres service name (`postgresql-x64-NN`) and set `DependOnService` so the app waits for it at boot; add `AppExit Default Restart` + `AppRestartDelay 5000` + `AppThrottle 10000` so a crash auto-recovers without hot-looping; same restart policy on the Tunnel service (throttle left low for its existing fast-reconnect startup path). Synced `docs/windows-services.md` and added a power-loss cold-boot test + UPS recommendation to `docs/go-live-checklist.md`. Note: the daily `DreamyCafe DB Backup` scheduled task is a documented manual setup step (`docs/database-backup.md`) — verify it exists on the mini PC via `scripts/verify-resilience.ps1`.
- **Hardened deployment for 24/7 running: pinned timezone + log rotation** — a review of running the app as an always-on server surfaced two deploy-config gaps (the app code itself is 24/7-safe: no memory leaks, self-pruning rate limiter, lazy stale-order sweep, `inFlight` reset in `finally`, PostgreSQL, production `next start`). (1) **Timezone:** the app derives "today" for every day-boundary report (today's sales, Z-report, daily breakdowns) from the server clock, but nothing pinned it — a box on UTC (the cloud/Linux default) would report the wrong business day during evening/overnight trade. Demonstrated live: `TZ=UTC` reported day `2026-07-05` while `TZ=Australia/Sydney` correctly reported `2026-07-06` at the same instant. Fixed by pinning `TZ=Australia/Sydney` in the NSSM App service (`scripts/install-windows-services.ps1`, `AppEnvironmentExtra`), documenting the shop-timezone requirement as a host-setup step in `DEPLOY.md`, and syncing `docs/windows-services.md`. (2) **Log rotation:** the App service rotated at only 1 MB (churns constantly) and the Tunnel service had **no** rotation (unbounded growth over months). Raised App rotation to 10 MB, added `AppRotateOnline 1` + 10 MB rotation to the Tunnel service. No app code changed.

### Fixed
- **Order history / Sales / Reports showed the wrong day near midnight (UTC-vs-local day bug)** — the whole date pipeline conflated UTC and local calendar days, so on a shop PC in a non-UTC timezone, querying "today" just after local midnight returned the wrong day's orders (reproduced at 00:xx AEST: `?date=<local today>` returned 0 because the client sent the UTC day and the server built a UTC-skewed window). Root cause: (1) client helper `formatDateForApi` (`src/lib/formatters.js`) emitted the **UTC** day via `toISOString()`; (2) server boundary logic did `new Date("YYYY-MM-DD")` (parses as **UTC** midnight) then `.setHours()` (shifts in **local** time), duplicated inline across ~6 routes; (3) report grouping bucketed by `createdAt.toISOString().slice(0,10)` (**UTC** day), disagreeing with the local-day range. Fixes: `formatDateForApi` now returns the **local** wall-clock day; added string-safe local-day helpers `startOfLocalDay`/`endOfLocalDay`/`localDayKey` in `src/lib/accounting.js` (parsing `"${dateStr}T00:00:00"` as local midnight) and routed `parseReportDateRange`, `todayApiDate`, the daily grouping (`aggregateAccounting`), and the orders CSV date column through them; replaced the inline boundary pattern in `orders`, `sales`, `clock`, `reservations`, and `cash/movements` GET routes with the shared helpers. The **Z-report was already correct** (it filters by the cash session's real `openedAt`/`closedAt` timestamps, not a date string) and is unchanged. Verified against the live app at the exact local/UTC-day boundary: a new order now appears under `?date=<local today>`, the reports `dailyBreakdown` buckets it into the local day (matching the range), and the Z-report totals are unaffected.
- **POS could save negative-total and empty orders** — `POST /api/orders` computed `total` from client-supplied discounts/quantities with no floor and no items check, so an over-discount or a negative quantity persisted a negative-total COMPLETED order, and an empty `items` array saved a $0 order — both corrupting the till and reports. Now the route rejects empty/missing `items` with `400 "Order must have at least one item"`, and floors the computed `total` at $0 via `max(total, D(0))` (a 100%+ discount becomes a free order, never negative); `cashAmount`/`amountPaid`/`change` derive from `total` so they follow. Scoped to the trusted staff-LAN path as a safety floor (the online path already recomputes/validates independently). Verified: over-discount and negative-qty orders now save `total = 0.00` (HTTP 201), empty/missing items return 400, and normal discount+surcharge+tip math (`8.95`) is unchanged.

### Changed
- **A product's own add-ons now override its category's, instead of stacking on top** — previously the add-on modal merged `ProductModifier` + `CategoryModifier` unconditionally, so a Milkshake in the *Cold Drinks* category showed every category add-on (Lime, Strawberry, …) even ones that don't fit it. New rule: **if a product has any of its own modifiers, the inherited category modifiers are suppressed for it**; a product with no own modifiers still falls back to the category list. This lets you curate per-product add-ons just by adding them to the product — no manual re-entry of the shared ones, and no schema/sub-category needed. Centralized the rule in a new `src/lib/productModifiers.js` (`effectiveModifiers`) and applied it everywhere the two lists were merged: POS `ProductOptionsModal`, `ComboBuilderModal`, the public `PublicOnlineOrderPage`, **and** the server-side online validator `src/lib/onlineOrderItems.js` (so the recompute-server-side zone rule still holds — a stale client can't submit a now-suppressed category add-on). Verified against the live DB: for a product with both own and category modifiers, the online validator now accepts an own modifier and **rejects** a category modifier (`Invalid modifier`), while a product without own modifiers still accepts its category ones.

### Fixed
- **Purchasing receiving let you type `e`/`+`/`-` in the Qty (and Unit $) fields** — the `New Receiving` line inputs are `type="number"`, and browsers allow the scientific-notation `e`/`E` plus `+`/`-` characters in those, so a stray letter visibly sat in the Qty box (the `parseInt`/`D()` parse then silently dropped it). Added an `onKeyDown` guard on both line inputs in `src/app/admin/purchasing/page.js` that blocks `e E + -` (and `.` on Qty, which is integer-only), so only valid digits can be entered. Verified the page compiles and serves 200 after the change.

### Changed
- **Admin (Owner) session now slides while the panel is open, instead of hard-expiring at 5 min mid-task** — the admin session is a deliberately short-lived 5-minute JWT (`ADMIN_TTL`, cookie `maxAge: 5 * 60`), so a long in-panel job (closing stock count + payroll) could expire *while the owner was still working*, making admin-gated saves silently 403 and popping the PIN modal mid-task. Added a sliding-session heartbeat scoped to the Admin panel: a new `PUT /api/auth/admin` re-mints the token, but **only when the current token is still valid** (missing/expired → returns `null`, sets no cookie), so it can extend a live session but never resurrect a dead one — the "self-expires, re-prompt on entry" security intent is preserved. The Admin layout (`src/app/admin/layout.js`) drives the heartbeat every 3 min via the existing `usePollWhenVisible` hook, which pauses when the tab is hidden. Result: while the Admin tab is open and visible the session renews indefinitely (no PIN re-entry, no lost work); once the owner leaves for POS/Manager, closes, or backgrounds the tab, renewal stops and the last 5-min token lapses so returning to `/admin/*` re-prompts for the PIN. No change to `ADMIN_TTL`, Manager/POS sessions, schema, or dependencies.

- **Documented: customer menu (`/order`) stays edge-cacheable by design — not a bug** — investigated whether the same cache header that caused the stale POS menu could cause a stale customer *price* → wrong charge. Traced the full online-order path and confirmed it **cannot**: `POST /api/orders/online` re-resolves every `unitPrice` from the live DB in `resolveOnlineOrderItems` (the client sends only product ids/qty/size, never prices), so the Stripe/Square charge is always the current price even if the customer briefly saw a stale one. The only effect of the ~60s stale customer menu is a cosmetic price/name lag that self-heals — kept intentionally so Cloudflare can edge-cache the public menu on the single shop PC. Recorded this decision as a bullet in AGENTS.md (Public/Private Zones) so `usePublicMenu` is not "fixed" with `no-store` later; the staff POS `useMenu` correctly does use `no-store`.

### Fixed
- **POS showed a product under its OLD category (and stale name/price/emoji) after a manager edit** — changing Almond Cake's category (Tea→Breakfast) saved correctly (DB + API + manager list all showed Breakfast) but `/pos` kept it under the Tea tab. Root cause was in `src/hooks/useMenu.js` (POS-only): (1) it fetched `/api/products` + `/api/categories` **without `cache: 'no-store'`**, and those routes send a cacheable `Cache-Control: public, s-maxage=60, stale-while-revalidate=300` (for the customer edge), so the browser served a stale menu with the old `categoryId` — the same class of bug already fixed on the manager page but never on the POS hook (missed sibling call path); (2) `useMenu` fetched **once on mount** and its `refresh()` was never called, so an all-day till never picked up edits without a manual reload. Fixes: both fetches now use `cache: 'no-store'`; the POS drives the (now-silent) `refresh` via the existing `usePollWhenVisible` hook (60s + on tab focus). Ruled out (with evidence) the service worker (`public/sw.js` bails on `/api/*`) and the localStorage `dc_menu_cache` (read only when offline). Verified end-to-end against the running app: a no-store refetch reflected a live categoryId change immediately.

### Changed
- **Cursor rules now auto-sync from AGENTS.md (fixed drift)** — `.cursor/rules/dreamycafe.mdc` was a hand-maintained full copy of AGENTS.md that had drifted stale: it still described the old single-provider payment model (online = Stripe only, stamps accrue "in the Stripe webhook", no Square online checkout / `fulfillPaidOrder` / `onlineProvider`) and the older desktop launcher. Extended `scripts/sync-claude-md.js` so `npm run claude:sync` / `claude:check` now also regenerate/validate the `.mdc` = Cursor frontmatter + verbatim AGENTS.md body (marked generated, do-not-edit-by-hand). Regenerated it, so Cursor and Claude Code now follow the same up-to-date rules from the single canonical source, with no future drift.

### Fixed
- **Same stale-modal bug class swept from sibling call paths (POS checkout)** — after fixing the menu forms, an audit of every `Modal`-based form found the identical "seeds local state from a prop but is rendered unconditionally, so it never unmounts and shows stale data on reopen" defect in two more places, both on `/pos`: **`OrderAdjustmentModal`** (seeds discount/surcharge from `initial*` props via `useState` initializers that only run once at mount — so reopening the order-adjustment modal showed the *first* session's values regardless of what was applied since) and **`ComboBuilderModal`** (seed effect keyed on `[combo?.id, editKey]`, identity-blind — editing the same combo cart line twice showed the first edit's selections). Both fixed with the same structural pattern: gate the render (`{adjustmentOpen && …}`, `{selectedCombo && …}`) so the component unmounts on close, plus a remount `key` on the combo builder per edit target. Audit confirmed all other modals (Checkout, PIN, SwitchUser, MoneyInOut, Reservations, OrderDetail, PrintQueue, staff/users/reservations forms) are safe — they either reseed on `[isOpen]`, seed imperatively on open, or are already conditionally rendered.
- **Product PUT could lose all sizes if the update failed** — `PUT /api/products/[id]` did a bare `productSize.deleteMany` *before* `product.update`, outside a transaction, so a failed update (e.g. bad categoryId) left the product with its sizes deleted and none recreated. Wrapped both in `prisma.$transaction`, matching the combo PUT route which already did this.
- **Menu edits needing a second save to apply — STRUCTURAL root cause (the real one)** — a recurring bug where editing a product field (emoji/name/description/category), saving, and reopening showed the *old* value, so the change only "landed" on a second save. Earlier fixes addressed secondary layers (click-handler stale closures, swallowed save errors, HTTP cache) but not the cause. Root cause: `ProductForm` was rendered **unconditionally** in `src/app/manager/menu/page.js`, and `Modal` only returns `null` for its own wrapper — so `ProductForm` **never unmounted** and its `form` useState survived every close→reopen. Combined with `editProduct` never being reconciled to the reloaded list after a save, and a seed effect keyed on `product?.id` (identity-blind, so it couldn't tell the same-id product's *contents* had changed), the form re-seeded from a stale reference frozen at the moment Edit was first clicked. Fix: gate the modal render with `{formOpen && ( … )}` (unmounts on close, discarding retained state — the same correct pattern already used at `admin/users/page.js`) and add `key={editProduct?.id ?? 'new'}` so it remounts fresh per product; same treatment for `ComboForm`. Also `await loadData()` after save so the fresh list commits deterministically. This makes retained state and the id-only dep both structurally unable to go stale — the form is born fresh from current props each open.
- **Menu edits (emoji, name, etc.) appeared to need saving twice — stale HTTP cache on the manager refetch** — `GET /api/products` (and `/api/categories`) send `Cache-Control: public, s-maxage=60, stale-while-revalidate=300` for the customer edge/CDN, but that header applies on the LAN/manager path too. The Menu page's `loadData()` refetched with plain `fetch(...)`, so right after a save the browser could serve the **pre-save** list from its HTTP cache (`stale-while-revalidate` serves stale for up to 5 min while revalidating), making a correctly-saved change look unsaved until a second save/refresh landed past the cache. The DB was always correct — only the manager's read was stale. Fixed by refetching with `cache: 'no-store'` in `loadData()` for products, categories, and feature-settings. (The POS/customer menu hooks keep the cache intentionally — they don't mutate-then-immediately-reread.)
- **Emoji picker needed two clicks (and the 2nd applied the 1st pick) across product, combo, and category forms** — the emoji buttons (and the text inputs/checkboxes) used the stale-closure updater `setForm({ ...form, ... })`, which spreads the `form` captured at render time. Under React's render/commit timing this could overwrite the field with the previous render's value, so a pick only "landed" on the following click. Switched every interactive `setForm` in `ProductForm`, `ComboForm`, and `CategoryManager` to the functional updater `setForm((f) => ({ ...f, ... }))`, which always reads the latest committed state. Also added the missing `type="button"` to the category emoji/color buttons (defensive — they aren't in a `<form>` today, but `type` defaulting to submit is a latent footgun). This is distinct from the earlier `useEffect` reset-clobber fix; that addressed re-renders wiping edits, this addresses the click handler itself writing stale state.
- **Menu saves (product/category/combo name, description, etc.) silently reverted on any server error** — the Menu page's `handleSave`/`handleSaveCombo` and `CategoryManager.save` never checked the HTTP response, so a failed PUT/POST would still close the form and refresh the list, making a valid-looking edit "not stick" with no explanation (the same silent-failure class as the earlier delete two-click bug). All three now check `res.ok`: product/combo save throws on failure and the form (`ProductForm`/`ComboForm`) catches it and shows the real error inline instead of closing; category save shows the error in place. Also clarified the `ProductForm` seed effect's intent — it re-seeds on open (`[isOpen, product?.id]`) so reopening a just-saved product shows the latest values, while still never wiping an in-progress edit on an unrelated re-render. (Verified the product PUT persists name/description correctly end-to-end against the live endpoint; the reported symptom was the swallowed-error path, now surfaced.)
- **Couldn't delete a product/category that had ever been ordered; product delete silently "took two clicks"** — historical order snapshots referenced menu items via `Restrict` foreign keys, so any product, product-modifier, or category-modifier ever used in an order blocked deletion (surfacing as the generic "Failed to delete category/product"). And the Menu page's product/combo delete handlers never checked the response, so a blocked delete silently refreshed the list and looked like nothing happened. Fixes: (1) migration `product_order_history_preserve` — `OrderItem.productId` is now nullable with `onDelete: SetNull`, and `OrderItemModifier.modifierId`/`categoryModifierId` are `SetNull`, so deleting a menu item severs the link but **keeps order history**; (2) added `OrderItem.productName` snapshotted at order creation (in-store + online), with every historical read falling back to it (`item.product?.name ?? item.productName`) across receipts/dockets (`printer.js`), reports (`OrderHistoryTable`, `TopItemsChart`, `/api/reports` aggregation), sales/online/refund/detail modals, and the public track page (+ its API projection); (3) the Menu page now checks `res.ok` on product/combo delete and shows the real error via `usePromptDialog`. Verified end-to-end: a product + category modifier used in a completed order now delete cleanly and the order retains its item/modifier names.

### Changed
- **Switch User moved from under the POS category column into the top NavBar** — the cashier/Switch User button used to sit at the foot of the category sidebar; since it's rarely used it now lives in the top `NavBar` alongside the other occasional actions (Reserve/Print/Clock/More), styled as a `UtilityButton` with the cashier's initials as a badge. The switch-user modal state moved into `CashierContext` (`switchUserOpen` + `openSwitchUser`/`closeSwitchUser`) so the app-wide nav can trigger the POS-only modal without coupling to `pos/page.js` internals; the button only renders on `/pos` (not `/pos/loyalty`), since that's where the modal lives.

### Fixed
- **Stray horizontal scrollbar under the POS category sidebar** — the category column's scroll container (`pos-category-scroll` in `src/components/pos/CategoryTabs.js`) had `overflow-y-auto` but no horizontal control, so when the vertical scrollbar appeared it ate into the padded width and pushed the `w-full` buttons just past the box, rendering a short phantom horizontal scrollbar. Added `overflow-x-hidden` so only vertical scrolling remains.
- **`stop-pos.bat` missed a launcher-spawned Node server and gave ambiguous output** — `scripts/stop-pos.ps1` detected the Next.js server *only* by whoever was listening on port 3000, so a server still running migrations (`prisma migrate deploy`, not yet bound) or a wedged/orphaned `node.exe` was never killed, and the script silently skipped any category it didn't find (hence the confusing "only cloudflared" output when the server had already exited). It now also matches the server `node.exe` by command line (repo root / `next start` / `next-server`, deduped against the port-3000 kill and never its own PowerShell PID), and prints an explicit line per category — `Electron`, `Node server`, `cloudflared` — including a "not running (or managed by an NSSM service)" note so the output always explains itself. Still scoped to launcher-spawned processes; NSSM services are left untouched.
- **Cash drawer double-deducted refunds** — `computeExpectedCash` (`src/lib/cashSession.js`) summed cash sales with `status: 'COMPLETED'` only, so refunding a cash order both removed the sale from the sum **and** subtracted the refund's `CashMovement OUT`, dropping expected cash by twice the refund. Now includes `REFUNDED` (matching the z-report query and the documented convention in AGENTS.md): the sale stays counted and the OUT movement nets it out exactly once.
- **Customers could pay for an online order the stale sweep had already deleted** — the sweep removes `AWAITING_PAYMENT` orders after 1h, but the Stripe Checkout session had no `expires_at` (payable for 24h) and Square payment links never expire, so a late payment charged the customer with no order on record (the webhook's `fulfillPaidOrder` silently no-ops on a missing order). Stripe sessions now carry `expires_at` = 1h (matching `STALE_ORDER_MS`); the sweep verifies payment per order first (rescuing paid-but-unfulfilled orders via `fulfillPaidOrder`) and deactivates Square payment links (`deactivateOnlineCheckout` in `src/lib/onlineCheckout.js`) before deleting — a failed deactivation keeps the order for retry next sweep.
- **Stale-sweep race could release reservations for a paid order / double-release stock** — the sweep released voucher/stamp/stock holds *before* deleting orders and never cleared them, so a payment landing in between left a PENDING order whose holds were given back, and a failed delete re-released stock on the next sweep. The sweep (and the checkout-failure path in `POST /api/orders/online`) now deletes the order row conditionally (`status: 'AWAITING_PAYMENT'`) first and releases holds only when the delete actually claimed the row; each stale order is processed in its own try/catch so one failure can't abort the sweep.
- **Tables page line totals used raw float math and omitted add-ons** — `src/app/tables/page.js` rendered `item.unitPrice * item.quantity` (string × number coercion on API money, banned by the money rules) and ignored modifier prices, so lines with paid add-ons displayed less than their real share. Now uses `lineTotalForItem` from `src/lib/orderTotals.js`.
- **Pickup name containing "|" corrupted the stored customer phone** — the online order note is `Pickup: name | phone`, and `parsePickupNote` splits on the first `" | "`, so a pipe in the customer's name made fulfillment parse a junk phone and create a bogus Customer row. The name is now sanitized (pipes stripped, whitespace collapsed) before it's embedded in the note.
- **Rate limiter buckets never pruned** — `src/lib/ratelimit.js` kept every expired per-IP/per-code bucket forever (slow unbounded memory growth on a long-running server). Expired entries are now swept lazily every 10 minutes inside `rateLimit`.
- **Product emoji (and other edits) needed two clicks to stick** — `ProductForm`'s reset `useEffect` depended on `[product, isOpen, categories]`, all reference-compared, so any parent re-render that produced a fresh `categories`/`product` reference re-ran the effect and reset the whole form, discarding an in-progress emoji pick. The reset now runs only when the modal opens or the edited product's id changes (`[isOpen, product?.id]`), and no-ops while closed.
- **Category emoji picker only offered 12 icons while Products/Combos offered ~30** — `ProductForm`, `ComboForm`, and `CategoryManager` each hardcoded their own `EMOJI_OPTIONS` array, so a category like "Breakfast" couldn't use 🥯/🍳 even though products could. Unified into one `MENU_EMOJI_OPTIONS` constant (`src/lib/constants.js`, the de-duped union of all three lists) used by all three pickers.

### Added
- **Add-ons / modifiers can now be created during product creation** — previously the Add-ons section only appeared after a product was saved (modifiers attach to a product id, so the live `ModifierManager` had nothing to POST to). New `DraftModifierManager` (`src/components/admin/menu/DraftModifierManager.js`) buffers add-ons in the create form's local state; `POST /api/products` now accepts an optional `modifiers` array and creates them via Prisma nested-create alongside the product (same pattern as `sizes`). Editing an existing product still uses the live, immediately-persisted `ModifierManager`. Also fixed the product POST route silently dropping `loyaltyEnabled` (the form already sent it; only the PUT route saved it).

### Changed
- **Agent tooling: rule-enforcement hook + gitignore cleanup** — added a Claude Code `SessionStart` hook (`.claude/settings.json` + `.claude/hooks/inject-rules.js`) that re-injects the critical AGENTS.md rules (JS-only, decimal.js money, no native dialogs, no new deps, changelog, public-zone recompute) at the start of every session so they can't be skipped; AGENTS.md stays canonical. Fixed a `CLAUDE.md` sync drift (trailing newline) so `npm run claude:check` passes. Replaced the stale `/.codebase-memory/` gitignore entry (tool never built, no code references) with `/.codegraph/` for the code-index MCP used as external dev tooling. None of this touches the app runtime or dependencies.
- **Agent-tooling policy in the session hook (codegraph + headroom)** — the `SessionStart` hook now also states the external-dev-tooling policy: codegraph (read-only code-index MCP) is fine to use anywhere; the headroom context compressor must NOT be used while working on money/payment/auth/zone code (`src/lib/money.js`, `src/app/api/orders|terminal|webhooks/**`, `src/proxy.js`, `src/lib/zone.js|paymentProvider.js|onlineCheckout.js|fulfillment.js`) because lossy compression there can drop a `D()`/`toCents()`/`cmp()` call site or a sibling call path. Standing-instruction guardrail only — both tools are external and add nothing to the app or its dependencies.
- **Superpowers scoping (mirror of the headroom restriction)** — if the Superpowers spec/plan/TDD methodology plugin is installed, the hook now scopes it to the same money/payment/auth/zone file set as the headroom restriction: use its spec-plan-review-TDD workflow only there; everywhere else stay in this repo's lazy-senior-dev mode (shortest working diff, no unrequested spec/plan ceremony) since Superpowers' default process is heavier than AGENTS.md calls for on routine fixes.

### Fixed
- **Expanded emoji fallback picker** — product/combo emoji options now include more food icons for common cafe items (sandwiches, soup, noodles, wraps, breakfast, etc.) so fallback icons better match menu items when images are off/missing.
- **iPad POS responsiveness** — memoized product grid cards so cart updates do not re-render every menu tile; lazy-load product images; slowed background polling (health, online orders, print queue, sessions) and pause polls when the tab is hidden; stabilized context values to cut unnecessary re-renders.
- **iPad Online Orders column layout** — `/online` used a 2-column grid between 768px and 1280px, so on iPad New and Ready stacked in one column; now always 3 columns (New | In Progress | Ready) with per-column scroll.
- **iPad POS category swipe rubber-band** — category sidebar had no scroll container, so iOS shifted the whole app when swiping the menu; category list now scrolls in place (`overflow-y-auto`, `pos-category-scroll` touch rules).
- **LAN tablet login redirected to localhost** — POST-login and proxy redirects used `request.url`, which Next.js reports as `localhost` even when the tablet connected via `192.168.x.x`; redirects now use the client `Host` header (`requestPathUrl` in `src/lib/requestUrl.js`).
- **iPad POS crash after login on LAN HTTP** — `usePosLock` called `crypto.randomUUID()`, which browsers only expose in secure contexts (`https://` or `localhost`). Tablets hitting `http://192.168.x.x` threw `crypto.randomUUID is not a function` and blanked the till; fixed via `randomId()` helper (`getRandomValues` fallback). Same fix for combo cart lines in `useCart`.
- **Sales History excludes unpaid online checkouts** — `GET /api/sales` omits `AWAITING_PAYMENT` orders by default (unpaid Stripe/Square sessions); the summary total also skips cancelled rows. Use Manager → Sales → Online Orders for in-flight/unpaid online orders.
- **Public order path security hardening** — closes race conditions and privacy leaks on the internet-facing `/order` surface. Online checkout now **atomically reserves** voucher usage, free-coffee stamp redemption, and tracked stock at `POST /api/orders/online` (new `onlineReservationsHeld` on `Order`; released on stale `AWAITING_PAYMENT` cleanup or checkout failure; fulfillment skips re-applying those side effects). `returnBase` is allowlisted to `/order` and `/onlineorder` only. Public `GET /api/customers` no longer returns another customer's name/phone (stamp progress only). Public `GET /api/products` omits combos, stock, printer routing, and combo slots. Voucher validate ignores client `customerId` on the public zone and adds per-code rate limiting.
- **Stock writes 403'd when entering the Owner panel by Admin PIN alone (Admin/Manager split fallout)** — `/admin/stock` and the `/admin/shift` end-of-shift stock count are Owner-panel pages gated by `admin_session`, but the `/api/stock-items` write routes still keyed off the **manager** cookie from before the panel split: `POST` used `verifyAdminRoleViaManagerCookie` and `PATCH`/`DELETE` used `verifyManagerAccess`. Since an Admin PIN grants entry to `/admin/*` **without** a manager session (the documented decoupled flow), every stock add/edit/delete and the shift stock count returned 403 for an owner who hadn't also logged into the Manager panel — and it broke the proxy's own invariant that "every `/admin/*` data API is independently ADMIN-gated". Switched the routes to accept the admin session while preserving role intent: `POST`/`DELETE` (add/remove) → `verifyAdminAccess` (ADMIN: `admin_session` or manager-ADMIN), `PATCH` (quantity) → `verifyAdminOrManagerAccess` (MANAGER or ADMIN, either session). `verifyAdminRoleViaManagerCookie` is now unused. (Audit of the Admin/Manager split found this was the only API still mis-keyed; proxy gates, session contexts, and all other Owner-panel APIs were already consistent.)
- **Managers can now manage add-ons / modifiers (was silently Owner-only)** — a Manager-role login could edit everything about a product (name, price, sizes, availability) and even delete it — the `/api/products/[id]` routes have no role gate — but the **Add-ons / Modifiers** section never appeared for them, so add-ons looked "missing". Root cause: the shared modifier API (`src/lib/modifierRoutes.js`) gated create/update/delete on `verifyAdminRoleViaManagerCookie` (ADMIN only) and both editors hid the UI behind `isAdmin` (`ProductForm`, `CategoryManager`), an inconsistency with the rest of menu management. Switched the modifier routes to `verifyManagerAccess` (valid `manager_session` with MANAGER **or** ADMIN — still a real auth floor, and stricter than the un-gated product routes) and dropped the `isAdmin` condition on the two add-on sections. Add-ons still require a **saved** product/category (they attach to its id), so the section appears once you save and re-open — there's no place to attach them on a brand-new unsaved record.

### Changed
- **iPad post-login POS load** — staff login now uses a full page navigation to `/pos` so Safari sends the new session cookie reliably; service worker no longer pre-caches `/pos` (auth-gated) and only stores successful HTML responses; SW registers on `/login` so the first till load does not race install + reload.
- **LAN tablet connectivity helpers** — `scripts/test-lan-pos.ps1` checks localhost + LAN IP reachability from the shop PC; `scripts/allow-lan-pos.ps1` (admin) consolidates the inbound TCP 3000 firewall rule. Staff quick reference adds iPad troubleshooting (full `http://` URL, same Wi‑Fi, iOS Local Network permission for Safari).
- **README brought up to date** — payments (Stripe + Square, per-channel providers, brand block Stripe-only), removed stale kitchen/front display mention, Manager vs Owner panels and sessions, combos/PWA/Electron/offline cash, Square `.env` vars, launcher scripts (`open-pos.bat`, `stop-pos.bat`), and expanded dev/test script table.
- **Counter reader — Stripe/Square tabs on Admin → Payments** — the Counter reader card no longer stacks both provider fields at once. A segmented **Stripe / Square** tab (with an “Active” badge on the in-store provider) shows one provider’s setup at a time; **Label**, **Enabled**, and **Save reader** stay shared and still persist both `stripeReaderId` and `squareDeviceId`. Each tab has its own **Test charge $1.00** button that exercises that provider’s reader without switching the global in-store provider (`runTerminalCharge` + `/api/terminal/charge|status|finalize` accept an optional `provider` override; POS checkout unchanged).
- **Hardened the public order path against L7 floods** — the internet-facing endpoints had almost no abuse protection: `POST /api/orders/online` (which writes an order and calls Stripe/Square per request) was unthrottled, the menu reads and `/api/health` were unbounded, and the rate limiter keyed off the spoofable first `X-Forwarded-For` entry. Changes: `clientIp` (`src/lib/ratelimit.js`) now prefers the un-forgeable `CF-Connecting-IP` (falls back to XFF/`x-real-ip` on LAN/dev). `POST /api/orders/online` gets a per-IP limit (5/min) plus a process-wide in-flight cap (`MAX_INFLIGHT = 8`, returns 503 when exceeded) so a distributed flood can't exhaust the DB pool or our shared payment account, and it now sweeps abandoned `AWAITING_PAYMENT` online orders (older than 1h, throttled to once/5min, cascades to items) so junk can't accumulate. `GET /api/products`, `/api/categories`, and `/api/health` are rate limited **only in the public zone** (`isPublicZone`) so the LAN POS menu/connectivity polling is never throttled; the menu responses also send `Cache-Control: public, s-maxage=60, stale-while-revalidate=300` so a Cloudflare Cache Rule can absorb menu floods at the edge (no `max-age`, so manager edits still show on the till immediately). Note: app-side limits are defence-in-depth — the first line against a flood is still a Cloudflare WAF rate-limiting rule / Under Attack mode at the edge, since all traffic funnels to the single shop PC.

### Fixed
- **Square online orders stuck on “Setting up your order…”** — Payment Link checkouts leave the Square order in `OPEN` (not `COMPLETED`) after payment; poll verification wrongly required `order.state === 'COMPLETED'`, so the success-page fallback never promoted the order. Now verifies via `payments.get` on the tender’s `paymentId` with `status === 'COMPLETED'`. Square `payment.updated` webhooks nest the payment at `data.object.payment` — the handler read `data.object` directly, so webhooks were ignored even when configured. — add-ons (product and category modifiers) were hidden in Menu Management unless the logged-in user had ADMIN role, and modifier mutations required `manager_session` with ADMIN only. Managers now see and edit add-ons like the rest of the menu (`ModifierManager` / `CategoryModifierManager`; `modifierRoutes.js` uses `verifyManagerAccess`). Admin → Stock add/delete (and inline quantity edits) failed when the Owner used only the short-lived `admin_session` from the Admin PIN — `POST`/`DELETE` now use `verifyAdminAccess`; `PATCH` uses `verifyAdminOrManagerAccess`.
- **Product images now display in production** — uploaded product photos showed as broken images after build. Root cause: the upload route writes files to `public/uploads/products/` at runtime, but Next.js serves `public/` as a build-time snapshot, so files added after `next build` 404 under `npm run start` (the Electron production launch) — it only worked in `next dev`. Added a route handler `src/app/api/uploads/products/[filename]/route.js` that reads the file from disk (with a `^product-…\.(jpg|png|webp|gif)$` filename guard, correct `Content-Type`, and immutable caching), plus an `afterFiles` rewrite in `next.config.mjs` mapping `/uploads/products/:filename` to it. Stored URLs and the public-zone proxy rule are unchanged; dev still serves the live file directly. This also fixes customer-facing product photos on `/order` in production.

### Added
- **Product images are now an optional feature (Admin → Settings)** — new `productImagesEnabled` flag on `FeatureSettings` (default **on**; migration `add_product_images_setting`) with a "Product Images" toggle on the Settings page. When **off**, `GET /api/products` nulls each `imageUrl` so the emoji icon shows everywhere (POS, manager, customer menu) while the stored photos are kept for if it's turned back on, and the product editor (`ProductForm`) hides the upload block and just shows the emoji picker (relabelled "Icon"). Wired through `getFeatureSettings`, `PATCH /api/feature-settings`, and the menu page passing `imagesEnabled` to `ProductForm`.

### Changed
- **Browser-print reports are now clean, branded documents (Electron-safe)** — the Owner **Print** buttons used to call `window.print()` on the live page, so the printout captured the whole app chrome (nav bar, Reserve/Print/Clock buttons, sidebar) and clipped long tables to one screen (`<body>` is `h-screen overflow-hidden`). An earlier popup-based attempt (`window.open`) silently failed in the Electron POS shell, which denies all popups (`setWindowOpenHandler(() => ({ action: 'deny' }))`). Replaced with a **popup-free, same-window print system**: a shared `PrintReport` component (`src/components/shared/PrintReport.js`) renders a hidden, fully-styled report into a `document.body` portal (escaping the app's `overflow-hidden`/`relative` shell), and a global `@media print` framework in `src/app/globals.css` hides everything except `.print-report`, restores page flow so tables paginate, and provides branded styling (DreamyCafe header, date range, generated timestamp, totals) with named-page orientation (`@page landscape`/`portrait`). Wired into **Payroll** (`/admin/payroll`, landscape) and the **Stock Report** (`/admin/stock`, portrait); each just supplies a plain `<table>` and calls `window.print()`. Thermal receipt/docket printing (ESC/POS) is unaffected.

### Changed
- **POS checkout surcharge/discount — bigger touch toggles + percent default** — the `%`/`$` toggle buttons and their value inputs in `CheckoutModal` Surcharge/Discount rows are now larger touch targets (`px-5 py-3 text-base` buttons, `py-3 text-base` inputs). Manual **discount now defaults to PERCENT** (matching surcharge) instead of FLAT — updated the defaults in `CheckoutModal` (state, prop, reset), `OrderAdjustmentModal`, and the `orderDiscountType` source/reset in `pos/page.js`. An existing cart-level discount still re-opens with its saved type.
- **`start-pos.bat` now launches with no visible terminal** — double-clicking `start-pos.bat` used to leave a `cmd.exe` console open the whole session (it runs `npm run build` then blocks on `call npm start`). The bat now self-relaunches hidden via a tiny dependency-free helper (`start-pos-hidden.vbs`, window style `0`) and re-enters with a `hidden` arg, so only the Electron splash/kiosk window shows. Because there's no console to read, build/install output now goes to `logs\startup.log` and failures `exit` instead of `pause` (which would hang invisibly). Electron's spawned `npm run start`/`cloudflared` children already use piped stdio, so they never opened their own windows. `start-pos.bat` is the single default launcher.

- **Expenses + Suppliers/Receivings → real P&L (toggle-gated, default OFF)** — two independent Owner features that turn the accounting summary into a true profit & loss. New `FeatureSettings` singleton (`expensesEnabled`, `purchasingEnabled`, both default false) drives everything: `src/lib/featureSettings.js` (`getFeatureSettings`, safe defaults when the table is missing) + `GET`/`PATCH /api/feature-settings` (LAN-read, admin-write), with a new **Admin → Settings** page (`/admin/settings`) of On/Off toggle cards. The admin sidebar always shows **Settings** and conditionally slots **Expenses** + **Purchasing** links based on the flags (data created while ON simply stops showing when toggled OFF; LAN APIs stay admin-gated regardless). **Expenses**: `Expense` model (`amount`, `category`, `note`, `spentAt`, optional `supplierId`) with admin CRUD `GET/POST /api/expenses` (+ `[id]` `PATCH`/`DELETE`, `parseReportDateRange` filter on `spentAt`) and an `/admin/expenses` month-range page (amount + category suggestions/custom + date + optional supplier + note; accounting-only — **not** deducted from the cash drawer, noted in the UI). **Suppliers + Receivings**: `Supplier`, `StockReceiving`, `StockReceivingLine` models; admin CRUD `GET/POST /api/suppliers` (+ `[id]`) and `GET/POST /api/stock-receivings` (+ `[id]` `DELETE`). Saving a receiving runs in a `prisma.$transaction`: it stores `totalCost = sum(lineCost)` and **bumps each line's linked `StockItem.quantity`** (sets it when currently null, else increments); deleting a receiving **reverses** those increments (clamped at 0). The `/admin/purchasing` page manages suppliers and builds multi-line receivings (pick a `StockItem` or custom name + qty + unit cost, live line/total cost) with a recent-receivings list. **Reports P&L**: `buildPnl` (in `src/lib/accounting.js`, gated by the flags) adds `purchasesTotal`, `expensesTotal`, `expensesByCategory`, and `netProfit = roundCents(netRevenue − purchases − expenses)` to `GET /api/reports/accounting`; `AccountingSummaryCard` renders a **Profit & loss** block (only when a flag is on) and `buildSummaryCsv`/`GET /api/reports/export` append `purchases_total`/`expenses_total`/`net_profit` rows. Migration `20260629143222_add_expenses_purchasing`. All money flows through the Decimal framework (`D`, `toDb`, `roundCents`, `sum`; strings over the API, rebuilt with `D()` on the client). Per-menu-item COGS / inventory valuation is documented as a future Phase B (recipe/BOM + costing engine), not built here.
- **Customizable combo components + in-cart combo editing** — combo components can now be customized at the till. `ComboBuilderModal` resolves each slot's chosen product and (from the live menu) lets staff pick a **size** and toggle **add-ons** per component; the upcharge is added on top of the fixed combo price (size upcharge = chosen size minus that product's cheapest size, so the cheapest size is "included" and upsizing costs the difference; add-ons add their own price). The builder shows a live total. Components and add-ons are still emitted as `source:'combo'` snapshot modifiers (now price-bearing) so `lineTotalForItem` and the server's `itemsSubtotal` already charge the upcharges with no API change; combos stay a single line and never earn per-component stamps/stock. Tapping a combo already in the cart **re-opens the builder pre-filled** (selections stored client-side on the cart line) and replaces it in place — new `useCart.addComboItem` (unique `cartItemId`, never merges) + `Cart` routes combo edits to the page-level builder. Customization stays POS-only.
